<meta>This page continues the discussion of refining wine into a Fifth Essence, now focusing on its use as a solvent to create "potable gold" for medicinal use. It emphasizes the distinction between natural gold and alchemical "made" gold, warning that the latter lacks healing properties.</meta>

# Of the Secrets of Nature

...also draw all its virtue or power out of it, so that it may pass into the **potable gold** <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; a liquid medicine containing gold, believed by alchemists to be a universal cure-all</note>. I am not against the fact that the ancients found many such ways to dissolve gold; however, they directed these methods more toward **alchemy** <note>the practice of transmuting base metals into gold</note> than toward **medicine** <note>original: *Artzney*</note>. For this reason, many have been deceived by this art, thinking that gold should be made out of such a **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the purest part of a substance, separated from its "gross" or physical matter</note>. And although this substance is stable in many respects and has the weight, sound, and luster of gold, it is nonetheless recognized as false in our times during a true test or proof. For if it is beaten into the thinnest leaves, dissolved in **strong water** <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to test gold's purity</note> with other metals, 

<margin>False gold.</margin>

or even tested through **antimony** <note>original: *Spießglaß*; a mineral used in the refining process to separate gold from other metals</note> (which is its highest test, whereby one recognizes the fabricated and foreign color most precisely), it cannot endure. And for this reason, **Arnaldus de Villa Nova** <note>a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist known for his works on wine and medicine</note> says: The wine in which a golden **plate** <note>original: *Lamina*</note> is quenched forty or fifty times is regarded by some as a potable gold. Therefore, **Johannes de Rupescissa** <note>a 14th-century Franciscan chemist who pioneered the medicinal use of distilled spirits</note> also says that the same wine becomes even better if one **calcined** <note>original: *calcinierte*; reduced to a powder through high heat</note> the aforementioned gold and made it into a very fine powder, or beat it as thinly as possible and cut it into small pieces. Since one might more easily draw out its powers in this way than if it were merely beaten into a plate. Yet, it should be gold found naturally in the earthly ore, and not gold made through any human cleverness or subtle arts. For such "made" gold has no power to sustain human life, but is rather a deception and a 

<margin>Alchemical gold is useless for medicine.</margin>

blinding of the eyes. For what alchemist can boast that he ever made gold without a poisonous substance (as is **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*; quicksilver, which alchemists believed was a base component of all metals, though highly toxic</note>)? And therefore (without doubt), the gold of the alchemists, although it has the color of true gold, does not have the virtue or power of the gold of the earth. For this reason, such a Fifth Essence of wine may quite well be drawn from the wine, according to the opinion and teaching of the ancients, whose method I will describe here (setting aside all other long-winded talk). Likewise, take gold and beat it as finely as possible, so that it may be dissolved into a gold-colored, drinkable form, which we may use by itself or with other medicines, as will be taught hereafter. Although another way has been found to dissolve gold, such as with **strong water** <note>original: *Aqua fortis*</note>, or with distilled vinegar, or with distilled spring water—of which it is not fitting to speak in medicine, because they are more **corrosive** <note>original: *Corrosiva*; substances of a "gnawing" or "biting" nature that can damage the body</note>, that is, of a gnawing nature, rather than being found useful and wholesome. And even if one could remove something of these sharp substances, as some believe or wish, it is nonetheless not certain and not quite believable; and even if it could be done, I still would not wish to use it. As our philosophers say: Between two uncertainties, choose the most certain and flee the most uncertain. For that reason, we wish to [pursue] a simpler and more...

<summary>This page warns against using "synthetic" alchemical gold for medicine, arguing that only natural gold processed with the Fifth Essence of wine is safe and effective. It critiques the use of corrosive acids like Aqua fortis in medical preparations, favoring gentler, "certain" methods.</summary>

->Philippus Ulstadius.<-

...show a more skillful way to separate the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*; the pure, celestial spirit of a substance, distinct from the four physical elements</note> from the four elements and to bring it into a single being through a sharpness mixed with subtlety. Through this, gold, silver, and other metals may be dissolved, and their power drawn out of them so that they may be turned into a drinkable form like water. Through this sharpness, the metals are dissolved and through nothing else, so long as the four elements are not yet divided or separated, and the Earth **calcined** <note>original: *gecalciniert*; a process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash," used here to prepare the element of Earth</note>, or turned by burning into a salt, lime, or ash, or made into the form of a stone, called by some of the ancients the *Lapis Philosophorum*, that is, the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and curing all diseases</note>. And there are two ways.

<margin>Two ways to separate the Fifth Essence from the four elements.</margin>

Something made with discretion from the most common substance is extracted from the aforementioned wine, in which there is great labor to separate it. I will not even mention (as I have taught before) that the subtle substance—by its own subtle nature, just as a soul is separated from the "gross" (that is, from the body)—may, through its subtlety, make a subtle thing even subtler and penetrate it. Through this, its powers and might are wonderfully increased. Therefore, in such an Essence or being, the power of any thing—such as gold, silver, and other metals, pearls, precious stones, herbs, and others of their kind—may be extracted; however, the gold cannot be dissolved this way.

The other way, however, is to separate the Air, Fire, and Water from the Fifth Essence and thereafter, through the **calcination** of the Earth, add the other qualities and **sublimate** <note>original: *Sublimier*; to purify a substance by heating it into a vapor and then collecting the condensed solid</note> them together through many circular distillations; in this way, the gold may be dissolved. You should understand this way or manner of making such an Essence in two ways. First, by what means the four elements are to be separated and how they may be brought back into one body. By this way, as some believe, the **Philosopher’s Stone** is composed, and through its secret and hidden power, all metallic ores may be dissolved for **tincturing** <note>original: *Tingiern*; the alchemical process of "coloring" or transmuting a base metal into gold</note>, dyeing, and **coagulating** <note>original: *Coagulieren*; the process of thickening or solidifying a liquid into a mass</note> gold, silver, metal, and other things. Secondly, how the Earth should be separated once the Fifth Essence has been drawn from it, through which one gains the power of the Earth. Through this also, gold, silver, and other metals may be dissolved and turned into the form of an essential drink, which may sustain human life and also perhaps refresh and restore it; it is to be used with the many virtues that it possesses.

# Of the Secrets of Nature #

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration of a distillation apparatus. It features a brick furnace with an arched opening at the bottom. A vessel (cucurbit) sits on the furnace, topped with an alembic (a helmet-like cap) that has a long delivery tube leading to a glass receiver flask resting on a tripod stand. To the left is a tall, vertical pipe or chimney composed of several segments.</image-desc>

Therefore, take the very best red wine you can obtain, and which is in no way adulterated, as we have said before. Then distill it three times in such a furnace as stands beside here. Some call this furnace the **Mary's Bath** <note>original: *Balneum Marie*; a water bath used for gentle heating, named after Mary the Jewess, an ancient alchemist</note>, yet there is a difference between this one and the other mentioned previously. Therefore, the uppermost part—that is the **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicus*; the cap of a still where vapors condense</note> or **helmet** <note>original: *Helm*</note>—which receives the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile alcoholic vapors</note> and gives them back again, should stand in lukewarm water. It should be so governed by that same water so that the wine one wishes to distill takes on less of any "fiery character" <note>original: *fewrige Complexion*; a harsh, burnt quality or over-concentration of the element of fire</note>, which would then be difficult to remove through many distillations, and is subsequently more harmful to human nature than useful. Thus, you may openly see the pipe, in which the wine is, passing through the warm water. 

But the other **Mary’s Bath**, mentioned above, has the lower part of the furnace shaped like a kettle, into which all the vessels and distilling glasses are to be placed. And the uppermost part, namely the **alembic**, should not stand in any water; rather, the **cucurbit** <note>original: *Cucurbita*; the bottom gourd-shaped vessel containing the liquid</note> itself, in which the wine is that one wishes to distill, is placed in the water. And that is the **Mary's Bath**. And whenever something is to be distilled in the first water bath of the kettle, one should provide themselves with large and small discs or leaden circles, upon which the glasses are tied, so that they are not pushed back and forth by the heat of the water bath 

<image-desc>A small woodcut of a distillation vessel, consisting of a rounded bottom part and a conical top (alembic), resting on a curved base or handle-like support.</image-desc>

and thus broken. And note, that in such a **Mary’s Bath**, the kettle should be built into the masonry, as you see here in the figure, and it should have on one side a long iron or copper pipe, into which the coals are thrown. These should glow under the kettle so that the heat from them strikes against the kettle, though they may not burn away in the pipe because there are many holes in the furnace and the pipe is enclosed. 

The **alembic** that stands in the water should also be made of copper and lined with tin on the inside, and the vessels should be cemented together at the top and bottom and best soldered at the joints on the outside, so that no water may escape anywhere. When the water becomes hot, open the tap at the bottom and let the hot water out, and put cold water in its place from the top; for by this, the **water of life** <note>original: *Aqua vite*; high-proof distilled alcohol or brandy</note> is improved in the **distillation** <note>original: *Distillation*</note>, and it draws less of the fiery and moist nature to itself. The apparatus or vessel in which the water is kept should be made of copper or brass, and be a hand's breadth or a little wider, and in...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the distillation of wine, specifically focusing on the sealing of the apparatus and the precise construction of the furnace to ensure controlled heat for the refinement of the spirits.</meta>

### Philippus Ulstadius ###

The middle should have a hole, and the circle around the outside should be two fingers thick and wide. It shall be placed upon the kettle that is in the furnace. Also, the hole of the kettle should not be larger than the hole in the **distillation vessel** <note>original: *Distillatorio*; the vessel where the substance is heated</note>. One should seal everything well with the **Lute of Wisdom** <note>original: *Luto Sapientiæ*; a specialized airtight sealing clay used by alchemists</note> or with a **lute** <note>original: *Luto*; a paste or cement</note> prepared with shearings of wool.

When you have placed your wine into the kettle in this manner, everything should remain sealed there until you have distilled a sufficient amount of the wine. For if you interrupt the process, you must thereafter have all the greater labor to recover what was lost, should you perhaps not have enough wine. For this reason, there is a tap in the lowermost kettle through which the water should be let out when the wine is distilled. When there is no more strength left in the wine, one should put more in through the small hole at the top. There, one should insert a funnel and fill the distillation apparatus, and afterward seal it very well so that no vapor may escape. One should seal it with a **luting** <note>original: *Leymen*; clay or lime</note> made with resin. You should also know that such furnaces are now seldom found among our **artists** <note>original: *Artisten* or *Künstnern*; referring here to alchemists and practitioners of the "Hermetic arts"</note>.

When you have distilled your wine, keep the **phlegm** <note>original: *flegma*; the watery, tasteless, and inactive liquid that remains after the spirit has been drawn off</note>, for more will be said of it hereafter. But place the wine thus distilled back into the **water bath** <note>original: *Balneum*; literally "bath," usually a vessel of warm water to provide gentle, even heat</note> of which we spoke at the beginning. Or, it may be done in a **Lazy Heinz** <note>original: *faulen Heinzen*; an "Athanor" furnace designed to burn for long periods with a self-feeding fuel hopper</note> with sand. If you do not wish to use a Lazy Heinz, then place it in a water bath where many distillation glasses are set together, each placed in its own **dish** <note>original: *Scherbe*; a ceramic container or shard used to hold the glass within the bath</note>, as is shown hereafter. But the disks should be contained in a ring or a tripod.

The water bath should be constructed so that the furnace is below in a housing, and it should be one-sixth or three-quarters of an **ell** <note>a historical unit of measurement, roughly the length of a man's arm</note> wide, and also have a copper lid. It should be made of unbaked bricks at the place where the coals are to be put, and two or three fingers thick, plastered all around in the very best way. The lid should also be made so that the heat rising up to it—when it is closed—may go back down to the base. One should seal it well everywhere so that it cannot easily lose the heat it has taken in. For this reason, the pipe should be atop the furnace, and a hand's breadth below the pipe there should be a grate so that the ashes may fall through to the bottom.

The furnace, however, should be square and have a **vent** <note>original: *Athemloch*; literally "breath-hole"</note> at each of the four corners through which the smoke may exit. The pipe should have a window a hand's breadth from the furnace through which the coals are thrown in, and it should always be kept very well closed with a special tool made for that purpose, which is as thick as the pipe itself. The fire should always be tempered or moderated through the four vents with certain **damper registers** <note>original: *stupff Registern*; sliding plates used to control airflow and temperature</note> useful and fit for that purpose. And thus the heat goes up through the pipe to the copper lid, and no one knows from whence it comes.

# IX #
### Of the secrets of Nature ###

Also, have a wooden vat or tub made the size of a waterwheel, which shall completely surround the copper lid. In this same vessel, one should put water so that the lid stands halfway deep within the vessel full of water. The **casting** <note>original: *guß*; likely referring here to the metal frame or the contents of the bath</note> shall be four or five **ells** <note>original: *Elenbogen*; a traditional unit of measurement roughly the length of a forearm</note> high, and two ells high from the bottom of the vessel there shall be a lid with many holes, so that each **cucurbit** <note>original: *Cucurbita*; a gourd-shaped chemical flask</note> may go into each hole. Upon each cucurbit, one shall set an **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum*; a still-head used for cooling and directing vapors</note>, and under each alembic a **receptacle** <note>original: *Receptackel*; a receiving flask</note> on the lid of the larger casting. And all things shall be well-sealed and **luted** <note>original: *verlutiert*; sealed with airtight clay or paste</note>, namely: the lid upon the vat or vessel, the cucurbits upon the holes of the lid, the alembics upon the cucurbits, and their receptacles thereunto. And thus you will have a sufficient heat and a hidden fire; and this is the form of the furnace as it is illustrated here.

<margin>
Recognition of when all the **spirits** <note>original: *spiriti*</note> are gone from the wine.
</margin>
If you wish to know when the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> have all been distilled and separated from the wine, look at the alembic; and when you see several drops trickling down, it is a certain sign that nothing more of the spirits remains behind. And as the water then rises up into the alembic, take the receptacle away from there and keep the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, inactive residue</note> that is in the cucurbit in one place. Fill the cucurbit again with **water of life** <note>original: *Aqua vite*; concentrated alcohol or spirits</note> and set an alembic upon it, and stop it up well with such a wax as you will learn of hereafter, so that no spirit may evaporate out. Distill it through the ashes in the **Lazy Heinz** <note>original: *faulen Heinzen*; an Athanor furnace designed to burn for long periods without tending</note>, though it would be better to distill it in **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a water bath or bain-marie</note>. If you wish to recognize when the spirits are all gone from the wine, this will always be known to you through the previous signs, or you may also take some of the **water of life** from the alembic and try it by taste to see if anything watery has remained behind—and this is more certain than certain. Therefore, when you have distilled it according to our teaching in **Mary's Bath** or in a **Lazy Heinz**, take the phlegm that remained in the distillation glasses and distill it eight or nine times, and always add the phlegm that remains back to the others. Do this as often as it takes until that same **water of life** is turned into a fiery substance, such that if a cloth is moistened therein and ignited, it burns away instantly along with the water. Also, if you were to put but a single drop of **olive oil** <note>original: *Baumöle*; literally "tree oil," the standard term for olive oil</note> into it, it quickly falls to the bottom and never comes up again, no matter how hard one shakes the glass. And thus you have the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*; the purest medicinal or spiritual extract of a substance</note> separated from the four elements. And mark very well how the joints and all the cracks around the alembic or

## Philippus Ulstadius ##

even if other vessels are sealed with tough clay, you should not cease to wrap them with three or four linen cloths. Note also that when the **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua vite*; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> is first put in, you will not distill more than one and a half **measures** <note>original: *Massen*; a traditional volume measurement, roughly 1-1.5 liters</note> out of ten. Everything that remains in the kettle is worth nothing and is called the **Phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, non-alcoholic residue left after distillation</note>; that is, water composed of half Air, Fire, and Earth. Thus, you should distill sixty measures, and you will have barely seven measures from the first distillation, barely five in the second, and in the third barely two or three measures. And so it goes: the more you distill, the less you will have, and that which is distilled last will be the purest, most subtle, and best of all.

<margin>Potable Gold</margin>
### To make a Potable Gold, in which you may also dissolve gold.<gloss>to make.</gloss> ###

But when you have distilled it three times, it will become most subtle, and therefore—unless you guard it very well—all its power will easily evaporate <note>original: *hinweg riechen*; literally "smell away"</note>. Therefore, when you wish to move it from one vessel into another, take a **strainer** <note>original: *Seihzeug* or *Colatorium*</note> and put it into the vessel into which you want to let the wine flow. Seal it all around with such a wax as will be taught hereafter. Set the other vessel upon it so that the neck is plunged right into the strainer, and in the same way seal the neck of the strainer all around, and then let the wine run from its cask into the other; in this way, its power evaporates less. The strainer should be a hand-breadth long.

<margin>
Sealing.
To make wax.
</margin>
<margin>
Distillation
through a
sponge.
</margin>
The method of making the aforementioned wax for **sealing** <note>original: *Verlutieren*; the process of hermetically sealing alchemical vessels with a "lute" or paste</note> is this: Take two pounds of **pine resin** <note>original: *Resina pini* or *Dannharz*</note> and melt it in an earthen vessel that is well-glazed on the inside. When it has melted, you should have another pot or earthen jug with clean water; lay a straw across it and pour the resin into the other jug, so that all its impurities settle in the straw. If it does not seem clean enough to you, take half a pound of **virgin wax** <note>pure beeswax</note> and two or three spoonfuls of **olive oil** <note>original: *Baumöls*; literally "tree oil," commonly referring to olive oil</note>, or as much butter if you do not have the oil. Melt it all together and stir it with a wooden splinter until it cools. Afterward, work it with your hand and prepare it fully. With this wax, one should seal all vessels and distillation apparatus in which one wants to keep a Water of Life. The vessels should be smoothed, leveled, and polished to the very best of your ability. Thereafter, one should lay a thin piece of glass, almost like a lid, upon it, and seal it again anew with the aforementioned wax. Note also that when such wine is distilled two or three times, you may take a fine sponge and cut it into pieces of such size that they touch the **cucurbit** <note>the gourd-shaped base of the still</note> everywhere at the top.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for distilling the "Water of Life." The previous page ended mid-sentence describing the sealing of the vessels; this page concludes that thought by explaining the use of a sponge to filter the distillate.</meta>

# On the Secrets of Nature #

...touch. And the same sponge should be tied with three or four hanging cords when you set the **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum*; the top part of a still, used for cooling vapors</note> on, in such a way that the sponge itself cannot fall to the bottom of the **distillation vessel** <note>original: *Distillatorium*</note>. One should dip the sponge in **olive oil** <note>original: *Baumöl*; literally "tree oil," usually referring to olive oil in this period</note> and squeeze it out again slightly, so that no oil drops from it into the **cucurbit** <note>original: *Cucurbita*; the gourd-shaped lower vessel that holds the liquid to be heated</note> and mixes with the matter that one wishes to distill. And when you have set the helm <note>the "head" or top of the still</note> on, seal it with the aforementioned wax. In this way, the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile alcoholic vapors</note> will distill perfectly through this sponge, but the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, non-alcoholic residue</note> will not be able to pass through because of the oil. And by this method, you will accomplish more in one distillation than in three others. However, the alembic should be made without a double rim, just as one makes "blind" helms <note>vessels without an exit spout, used for circulation/digestion</note>, except that these do have spouts <note>original: *Schnebel*; literally "beaks"</note>, unlike the blind ones, as you can well observe.

You may also make four chambers or seats, so that a cucurbit stands in each, into which the matter to be distilled is placed—namely, the wine distilled as described above—and thus you may reach the conclusion of your undertaking more quickly and easily. The pipe <note>the central chimney or flue</note> in the middle, however, should not be higher than the furnace, and this same pipe may be made square, of unbaked bricks. In each corner, there shall be a window four or five finger-widths wide and long. These should be made with a rim so that one may lower certain iron instruments or **registers** <note>original: *Register*; metal plates used as dampers to control airflow and heat</note> to temper the fire. These registers must be large enough to fill the entire rim of the window through which the heat might escape. This iron register shall have a post with many holes, each a finger's width from the next, so that you may hitch the iron upward as needed and stick a nail into the lowest hole when you desire a very strong heat. But if you desire a gentle heat for your work, then let the hitched iron down as you please. The form of these registers you see here in the depicted figure. You may also have registers made with three or four holes, such that the topmost is the smallest and the bottommost is the largest; the higher you lift the register, the more heat you will have, and thus you may make a large or small fire according to your will.

[A woodcut illustration of a chemical furnace built of brick masonry. Atop the furnace, four alembics on their cucurbits are arranged around a central taller vertical chimney flue. To the left and right of the top of the furnace, two separate diagrams show "registers" or dampers—metal plates with series of circular holes of increasing sizes. These registers are also shown inserted into slots on the sides of the furnace to regulate the fire. At the bottom center, there is an arched opening for adding fuel.]

This furnace is made for two reasons: First, so that one may make a large or small fire to distill quickly or gently, as you desire; Second, once it is heated, it does not easily cool down, so one does not waste much charcoal in vain. And note that the distillation vessels should not be placed in or taken out...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the distillation process. The previous page ended mid-sentence with "reach the conclusi...", which completes here as "[conclusi]on / unless they have first cooled down."</meta>

# Philippus Ulstadius #

...on, unless they have first cooled down. You may do this by lowering one of the **dampers** <note>original: *Register*; a sliding plate used to regulate the flow of air and heat in a furnace</note> to the bottom, yet the others will continue their work nonetheless. And when you set it back in, filled again with **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*; concentrated alcohol produced by distillation</note>, you may regulate the fire according to your pleasure until you have satisfied the requirements of the matter.

<margin>How the four elements should be separated.</margin>
But you should separate or divide the four **Elements** <note>the fundamental components of all matter in pre-modern science: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note> in this way: Take all the aforementioned waters that have been gathered together and place them in the kettle of the first furnace, or in a large **cucurbit** <note>original: *Cucurbita*; a gourd-shaped distillation flask</note>, or in several smaller ones if you cannot obtain a large one. Distill it in one of the aforementioned furnaces and draw the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, non-alcoholic part of the distillate</note> from it with a gentle fire, as if it were in **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a water bath or bain-marie used for uniform, gentle heating</note>, for as long as nothing more comes out. And when it has been distilled to the end, remove the **receiver** <note>original: *Receptackel*; the vessel that catches the condensed vapors</note> from there; you will find the phlegm in it. In the cucurbit or the kettle, a black matter remains, like a soft, liquid pitch.

However, for the sake of brevity, some put it into a vessel or iron boxes over a gentle **charcoal fire** <note>original: *Kolfewr*</note> until that same black matter looks like a thin pitch. Put this into a cucurbit. Afterward, put other phlegm into the box and do as you did with the first; then add the black matter to the other again. Do this as often as necessary until no more phlegm remains behind. Then put the black matter back into a cucurbit, set it in a **sand bath** <note>original: *Sandt*; a container of sand used to provide high, even heat to a glass vessel</note>, and draw the superfluous moisture out of it.

Afterward, pour in the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the most purified and potent spirit of a substance</note> which you previously drew off from it, to a depth of four or five fingers. Mix them well together and set them to **digest** <note>original: *Digerieren*; a process of gentle heating to encourage chemical breakdown or extraction</note> in Mary’s Bath. Afterward, distill it through an **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum*; the cap or "head" of the still where vapors condense</note>. When the water has been thus distilled, pour it back onto the **lees** <note>original: *Feces*; the solid remains or dregs at the bottom of the vessel</note> and distill it again. Digest it and distill it once more; the more often you do this, the better it will become.

<margin>Human Blood.</margin>
That which is distilled in the seventh distillation is called **Human Blood** <note>original: *Menschen Blut*; a symbolic alchemical term for the refined element of Air, not actual biological blood</note>, which the alchemists seek above all else, and this is the element of **Air**. Thus you have two elements elevated in the power of the Fifth Essence: namely, Air and Water.

Afterward, take the black matter mentioned above which remained in the distillation vessel and distill it in the sand bath through an alembic. Separate its **oil** from it, which is the element of **Fire**. When nothing more remains in it, you should keep the oil separately. You will then find a black matter at the bottom of the cucurbit; that is the element of **Earth**, from which one may make the **fermentation** to unite and sharpen the Fifth Essence. In that same Fifth Essence, one may dissolve **gold** and all other metals.

And when you see drops distilling in the alembic like a clear oil, remove the receiver and set a larger one in its place (as if you were making **strong water** <note>original: *Aqua fort*; likely referring to nitric acid, which requires heavy-duty receiving vessels</note>) because of the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*; volatile vapors</note> that enter it. You should not make a strong fire for twenty-four hours when the black matter begins to rise into the alembic and pass from it into the receiver.

D

These refined substances are intended for advanced operations, such as the dissolution of gold and other metals.</summary>

# Of the Secrets of Nature #

Toward the end, when no more oil appears, you should increase the fire until all the drops have passed out of the **alembic** <note>original: *Alembico*; the cap or head of a still where vapors condense</note>. Then, a black, dry, and burnt matter will remain at the bottom, which reeks of burning, just like any other scorched material. After this, take the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, inactive byproduct of distillation</note>, as was said above, and put it into the container, as has been frequently described. And when it has been consumed by many boilings, take the water that remains after the **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua Vite*; concentrated alcohol</note> has been distilled, once it has been purified, so that you have one pound of **feces** <note>original: *feces*; in this context, the solid dregs or dross remaining after distillation, not bodily waste</note> and four pounds of the aforementioned water. Mix them well together and put them into a glazed pot or jar. When the residue has settled to the bottom, strain it and pour another four pounds of the aforementioned water onto the residue. Do this repeatedly until all the stench is gone from the black matter. If you do not do this, you will have spent all your effort and expense in vain, unless you pour the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*; the most pure, concentrated, and medicinal core of a substance</note> over it and distill it back down again, as was said before.

The black earth, having been thus washed and purified, you should dry and grind into an extremely fine powder. Mix it with the two previously mentioned **rectified** <note>purified by repeated distillation</note> elements—namely Water and Air (which we previously referred to as "human blood" <note>This is a symbolic alchemical term for a specific red-colored distillate, not literal blood</note>)—and place it in **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneum Marie*; a water bath used for gentle, even heating</note> for three days and three nights, or slightly longer. Afterward, distill it again. The water that comes from this distillation is called **Rectified Fiery Water**. Thus you have three elements raised in the power of the Fifth Essence.

Next, take that same black earth and burn it in a **reverberatory** or **calcining furnace** <note>original: *Referber odder Calcinier Ofen*; a furnace where heat is reflected off the roof onto the material</note> until it becomes ash as white as lime. It would be even better, however, if you put it in a new pot and sealed a good lid onto it with the **Lute of Wisdom** <note>original: *Luto Sapientie*; a specialized airtight sealant made of clay, lime, and binders like cloth or hair</note> or one prepared with wool-scraps. Place it in a **lime kiln** <note>original: *Kalckofen*</note> for as long as one normally burns lime, which usually takes about fourteen days. In this way, you will be spared much cost and effort, because the matter does not always turn perfectly white in the reverberatory furnace, sometimes retaining a reddish tint from the fire if the heat in that furnace is not strong enough.

Afterward, when you take it out of the lime kiln, you can calcine it even better and more quickly in the reverberatory furnace, which previously might have been very difficult or slow for you. If it was not burned enough the first time in the lime kiln, put it back in. When it is sufficiently burned, mix it with the aforementioned fiery waters and let it stand for three days and three nights in Mary’s Bath. Then distill it again. Do this seven times, and calcine it in the reverberatory furnace just as many times. When it has been distilled seven times and calcined every time, you may test it: if you place some of this earth in the air to dry and then pour three or four drops of the aforementioned water upon it, and it does not dissolve <note>original: *zergeht*; refers to the earth not melting or turning into liquid upon contact</note>, then you have a certain sign that it is sufficiently prepared. But if it does dissolve, you must calcine and reverberate it again.

->Philippus Ulstadius.<-

also dissolve or melt in a **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a water bath or double boiler used for gentle heating</note>, as was mentioned. And do this repeatedly until the earth can no longer be dissolved; then it is properly prepared and is called **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua Vite*; here referring to the potent alchemical solvent</note> because gold is dissolved and refined through it. But this is truly the Stone (and it is not salt, as some say) which is called the **Rectified Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum rectificatus*</note>, or in German, the "justified stone of the wise masters." It is also called the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the most pure, concentrated, and medicinal core of a substance</note> separated from its four elements, in which a spirit is hidden, separated from its own kind. That is to say: it is separated from its **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, inactive byproduct</note> which is that same water; and from its oil, which is the Air; and from the fiery water, which is actually the Water; and from its burnt dregs <note>original: *Fecessen*</note>, which is the element of Earth.
<margin>The Philosopher’s Stone fixes all spirits and makes the gold perfect.</margin>
And the Essence, or the substance, thus reverberated, **calcined** <note>burned to a powder to remove impurities</note>, resolved, and again calcined in the aforementioned manner, is turned into a Stone with which one **fixes** <note>to make a volatile substance stable and solid</note> all spirits and makes them stable, for it has the highest power of spirituality and the lowest power of corporality. Therefore **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> says: "That which is above is like that which is below, and that which is below is like that which is above," and it is a single essence for making gold perfect and for dissolving all other kinds of metals. This is because it always retains its power and might, and contains the whole substance of the four qualities or elements in one being, in which the secret of all hidden secrets of nature is kept. And for this reason the Natural Master says: "The Stone rises from the four destructible qualities to the Heaven, which is indestructible, and it descends again from Heaven to the Earth." He indicated this with his previous words when he said: "The highest is that which is below, and the lowest is that which is above." Thus it has the power of the Fifth Essence to fix the metals that grow out of the earth. For the Stone is an **Elixir**, and is like a crystal, which perfectly contains within itself the **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note>, the mother of all metals. And when this same Fifth Essence is mixed with the mentioned Stone, and gold leaves are added to it, they are dissolved through the perfect penetration of the Stone. The remaining refinement hidden in the Fifth Essence is turned into a **Drinkable Gold** <note>original: *Aurum potabile*</note>, which then protects against many illnesses and restores health.

# Of the secrets of Nature /

We promised above to show two ways to divide the four elements and bring them back into one form, in order to dissolve gold, silver, and other metals. The first way has been sufficiently and abundantly explained; therefore, we now wish to show the second way: namely, how one should separate the Earth once the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the most refined and spiritual core of a substance</note> has been drawn out of it, through which one may then dissolve all metals and bring them into the form of a drinkable potion.

<margin>One may make a Water of Life to escape sickness.</margin>
Since all **philosophers** <note>The author refers to alchemists and natural historians</note> agree that one may make a **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aquam vite*; a highly concentrated medicinal alcohol or solvent</note> to sustain human nature and to flee all sickness, you should make a clear and **rectified** <note>purified through repeated distillation</note> Water of Life that burns away when lit, as we have said above. Under no circumstances use human blood, for the herbs and all kinds of **materials** <note>original: *Materialia*</note> placed within it lose their power because of the excess moisture that is in it, and they lose their good taste and smell; also, it would be contrary to human use. Do not make the "fiery water" either, for it has such heat and sharpness within it that all materials (except for metals) absorb this excessive heat. Thus, while you intended to make a **Water of Life**, which is a Water of the Living, a **Water of Death** <note>original: *Aqua mortis*; a corrosive or toxic acid</note> would likely result instead. Since, however, all power and perfection reside in that same Earth which is made into a Stone (as we have sufficiently described above), we add this Earth to our present Water, and make neither human blood nor "fiery water." In this way, it then becomes a true **Fifth Essence** or **Water of Life**, and not a **Water of Death**. The more often you **distill** <note>original: *Distillierst*; the process of vaporizing and condensing a liquid to purify it</note> it through the Earth, the more powerful and better it becomes. It should be noted that a small amount of this Earth has an immeasurably great effect or power in this work; just as a little yeast or sourdough starts a large batch of dough for baking, so also a tiny bit of this Earth builds up immense perfection and power, useful and beneficial for the human body. Therefore, one must beware of those who make the Earth from **tartar** <note>original: *Weinstein*; the acidic sediment from wine fermentation</note>, or who burn grape skins to ashes, or who use slaked **lime** <note>original: *Kalck*</note>, or vine ashes, or wood ashes, and pass these off as the Earth we are speaking of; this is simply not the truth. For **Geber** <note>original: *Geberus*; Jabir ibn Hayyan, a legendary 8th-century figure regarded as the father of alchemy</note> says it is a Stone and a **Medicine** <note>original: *Medicina*</note> that cannot tolerate any mixture of foreign things. Therefore, when you have made a Water of Life that has been **rectified** three or four times—so that it leaves no sign of moisture behind in the place where it is burned—separate the Earth from its **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, inactive byproduct of distillation</note> as quickly as you can. Put the phlegm into a kettle and boil it over the fire until it becomes as thick as melted honey. Then take it from the fire and let it cool until the next day, and you will find small

## The Twelfth Chapter / How one should extract the Fifth Essence from honey, which goes into the Potable Gold.

If you wish to extract the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*; the most refined, spiritual core of a substance</note> from honey, take a good portion of the very best honey from which the **honeycomb** <note>original: *Wesel*; the wax and structural parts of the hive</note> has not yet been removed, just as it comes out of the beehive. Make yourself three or four straining sacks, two cubits long and the width of the cloth. Put the honey with the honeycomb into them and hang them in the sun during the summer time. Place a clean vessel under each sack, into which the honey may distill out of its sack. In the winter time, however, make yourself a warm room, like a bath, and hang the sacks on the walls so that the honey melts or flows from the heat. When it has all become soft and liquid, put more into the sacks and do as you did with the previous batch. Continue this as long as you have enough, and from the **dregs** <note>original: *Fecessen*; the solid residue left behind</note> make wax, as is the custom. After that, you shall take six or eight earthenware vessels made of the very best clay possible, in the shape of...

# The Thirteenth Chapter: How one
# should extract the Quintessence 
# from *Chelidonia*—that is, 
# Celandine.

*Chelidonia* <note>Celandine or Swallow-wort</note>, according to the opinion of **Raymond Lully** <note>a 13th-century Majorcan philosopher and alchemist</note> and **John of Rupescissa** <note>a 14th-century Franciscan alchemist</note>, is understood to mean the same as *Coeli donum* <note>Latin: "Gift of Heaven"</note>, which is, in German, a "heavenly gift." But if we wish to look at the correct properties and the true interpretation of the name as it originated from the Greek language, its explanation must be spoken of differently. For **Pliny** and **Aristotle** say the name *Chelidonia* comes from the word *Chelidon* <note>Greek: χελιδών</note>, which in the Greek language means a swallow; from this comes the proverb: "One swallow does not bring the spring" <note>original: *Glentzen*; literally "shining" or "brightness," referring to the onset of spring</note>. It is with this herb that swallows restore the sight of their young in the nest. 

The herb, however, is of two kinds. The "Great" variety has a tall, rough stem and leaves broader than parsnips, of a whiteish color, and a lime-colored flower. The "Small" variety has leaves a little smaller than ivy and more rounded—not as white—and a yellow sap like saffron, which is sharp. The seed resembles poppy seeds. They turn green when the swallows come to the land and wither when they fly away. While they are still green, one takes them and presses the juice from them, and cooks it in an earthen pot upon hot ashes. It is used particularly against the...

->Of the Secrets of Nature<-
XVII

<margin>
Celandine juice is good for the eyes.
</margin>
...dimness of the eyes, and the juice by itself is used in eye salves or plasters, which are named **Chelidonia** <note>Greater Celandine (*Chelidonium majus*); traditionally used for eye ailments due to its yellow sap</note> after it. It also possesses countless other virtues and powers. For this reason, we have not without cause wished to speak of it in this book of ours, and to teach how one should extract the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*; the purified, spiritual core of a substance</note> from it, which also belongs in the **Potable Gold** <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; a liquid gold preparation believed to be a universal panacea</note>. Therefore, take **Chelidonia**, or Celandine, in the summer when it is fully ripe and green, and just beginning to turn white; cut or hack the flowers, roots, and herb—the entire substance together—as small as you wish to have it. Crush it in a stone mortar, then put it into a glazed earthen **cucurbit** <note>original: *Cucurbita*; a gourd-shaped vessel</note>. Seal it well, close it, and lute it <note>seal it with airtight paste</note>. Afterward, set it in fresh horse manure to ferment and digest for three weeks. Then, set an **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum*; a still-head used to collect vapors</note> upon it and distill it in **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneo Marie* (Bain-Marie); a water bath for gentle heating</note>. The fire should be mild. Stop up everything very well, and the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, inactive portion of the distillate</note> will come out, while the other three elements will remain at the bottom of the distiller. Then, pull out the **dregs** <note>original: *feces*; the solid residue</note> and rub them upon a marble stone as small and subtle as you can. Put them back into the vessel and pour the phlegm—which you drew off from the other three elements—back over them. Set a **blind alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum cecum*; a still-head without a spout, used to force vapors to circulate back down</note> upon it. Lute everything very well and set it to putrefy and digest for seven or eight days in **Mary’s Bath**. However, if you wish to avoid the expense, ferment it in horse manure. When this has been done, let the matter cool gradually. Afterward, set it in another furnace to distill in the ashes, and set an alembic with a snout upon it. Distill it just as was said regarding the separation of oil from the earth in the tenth chapter. Then a clear water will come out, which has a color like an oil, and it has within it two elements: namely, Air and Water. The other two, namely Fire and Earth, settle at the bottom. But if you wish to separate the phlegm from the Air, set the **Oil** <note>original: *Oleum*</note> in a new, fresh distiller and distill it in **Mary’s Bath** with a small fire; you will see the phlegm rise upward, and the **Oil** will sink down in the distiller. Thus you will have two elements, namely Air and Water, each separated from the other. For the **Oil** cannot rise nor be sublimated in **Mary’s Bath**, because the fire is not powerful enough. If you wish to separate the other two elements, take the dregs from which you distilled the **Oil**, and rub them on a marble stone as before. Then take one part dregs and four parts phlegm; mix them together. Then set it in the furnace of the water bath for six or seven days. Afterward, distill it through the sand with a great fire until a reddish water comes out. Distill it repeatedly until it is all turned into that same water; thus you will have two elements in it, namely Fire and Water, but the Earth will remain at the bottom of the **cucurbit** as a black matter. Afterward, put the reddish water into another **cucurbit** and set a snouted **alembic** upon it. Distill it in **Mary’s Bath**, so that the phlegm separates from it, and

# Philippus Ulstadius. XVII #

...remains a reddish oil <note>original: *Oleum*</note> at the bottom, which is the element of Fire. Thus you will have all four elements, each one specifically drawn out from the others. But as for the earthly element, **calcinate** it <note>burn it to a white ash</note> with a strong fire and force it thereby so that it may be well dried out; afterward, grind it again upon a marble stone and **imbue** it <note>moisten or soak</note> with the **phlegm** <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery distillate</note>. Distill it through an **alembic** <note>original: *Alembicum*; the cap of a still</note> until you see white little stones like a salt in the same matter. And dissolve or melt that same salt again with the water from which you distilled it; afterward, dissolve it again and distill it. Do this as often as necessary until the Earth loses all its impure and worldly color—namely, until it begins to turn white and is brought to such a whiteness as a wax—and then you have the **rectified** <note>purified</note> Earth.

But one might ask why the Earth settles at the bottom: this happens because the Air remains at the bottom, and the Fire may be drawn out through the Water as if through the phlegm, and through no other way. But if you wish to do this through an easier path, then dissolve each element with an equal part of its water; for instance, when you distill the phlegm in which the Air is, so that an equal amount of Air remains at the bottom, take again as much of the phlegm, distill it with the Air, and this shall happen seven times. But from the other phlegm, draw out the Air; from that which remains behind after the Air, draw out the Fire in the same way as we taught above. Afterward, distill it in **Mary’s Bath** <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a gentle water bath</note> and draw off the phlegm. Add as much of this watery substance to the red oil that previously remained at the bottom of the still as was just mentioned, and distill it also seven times. Afterward, wash the Earth with the phlegm, and through this way, each element will be more perfect in its power to fulfill its assigned effect.

<margin>
Another way to bring the four elements into the **Quintessence** <note>original: *Q. Essentiam*</note>.
</margin>

There is also another, even more subtle way to bring each element into its highest substance and into the **Quintessence**. However, it is assumed and intended to be understood that each element has been rectified beforehand. Afterward, one should put it into a **circulatory vessel** <note>original: *Circulatorium*; a vessel designed so that vapor condenses and flows back down into the liquid</note> and set it in horse manure or in Mary’s Bath for thirty days. After you have circulated it for thirty days, distill it again; then the body, as a coarse matter, will be turned into a **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritum*</note> and into the most subtle substance of all. Some work this through an easier way as follows: they take four parts of the Earth and one part from one of the four elements—whichever you wish—and **digest** <note>heat gently</note> it in the mentioned manner; they also circulate it for thirty days, and so each element turns into the Quintessence in that time because of the manifold rising and falling within the circulatory vessel. And if you wish to know when the matter has been circulated enough, look to see when the Quintessence floats on top of the other matter; then it has had enough.

# Of the Secrets of Nature #

...is the most [excellent] that you can make. Now it remains to be said what virtue or power the same extracted Quintessence <term>Quinta Essentia; the "fifth essence" or the purest, most spiritualized form of a substance</term> possesses. And therefore know that the element of Water, or the Phlegm <note>original: *Flegma*</note>, is useful for all hot and cold illnesses of the body if one takes a little of it morning and evening. Likewise, the Quintessence, Drinkable Gold <term>Aurum potabile; a legendary alchemical medicine consisting of gold in liquid form</term>, and the Water of Life <term>Aqua vite; a concentrated medicinal alcohol</term> are good for many illnesses. It also tempers <margin>For the heart.</margin> or moderates all the veins that are around the heart and drives out all poison from it. It heals all accidental ailments of the lungs; it purifies the blood and protects a person from all destruction of natural power; <margin>Lungs.</margin> it helps all sick people, regardless of what illness they may have. But the element of Air is like an Oil <note>original: *Oleum*</note> and has this power: it maintains young people in their strength and beauty <margin>Preserves youth.</margin> if they occasionally consume it in their food, for it allows no blood to rot. It burns away and drives out all salty phlegm and takes away melancholy <term>one of the four humors, associated with sadness and "black bile"</term>; furthermore, it carries away all the heat of choler <note>original: *Cholera*; referring here to the yellow bile humor, not the modern disease</note>. But the element of Fire has this power: if one crushes a piece of it as large as a grain of wheat and mixes it with a very good wine and pours it into the throat of a sick person (even one half-dead), it breaks through and revives all their lost <margin>Revives life.</margin> strength and power; for it penetrates to the heart, warms it, and drives all poison and superfluities away from the heart. If you wish to help someone who is already at their end and about to pass away—doing so in a twentieth part [of an hour] so that it is regarded as a miracle or a wonder—give them just one grain <note>a very small unit of weight</note> of this oil, which is the fiery element; they will recover in that hour, so that all onlookers will marvel at it and say it is a miracle or a great wonder-sign. And this is maintained and confirmed by Raymond Lully <note>a famous 13th-century philosopher and alchemist</note>.

<margin>Another way to extract the Quintessence from Celandine.</margin>
There are some, however, who extract the Quintessence from Celandine <term>Schellkraut; a plant used in medicine and alchemy, also known as Greater Celandine</term> by another way: they take it while it blooms, with the root and flowers, weigh it, and keep note of the weight for later; they chop it small and pour pure spring water over it, set it on a fire, and boil it until it reaches the [original] weight. Afterward, they pound it in a stone mortar and press the juice out through a straining cloth, remove the dregs <note>original: *feces*</note>, and boil the remainder until it becomes as thick as honey. And then the matter is ready to have the four elements separated from it, one from the other. Then they place it in a gourd-vessel <term>Cucurbita; the lower bulb of a still</term>, filling it, and seal <note>original: *verlutieren*; to seal with an airtight paste</note> a still-head <term>Alembicum; the upper cap of the distillation apparatus</term> onto it as best they can. They distill it in a water bath <term>Balneo Marie; "Mary’s Bath," a gentle heating method using a container of hot water</term> with a gentle fire, so that the water comes away from the matter. Afterward, they place the distilling vessel in ash <note>an ash-bath, which provides a higher, more even heat than water</note>, and another liquid separates from it, which is like an oil. And when for the third time they see the oil floating on top, they take away the receiving vessel <note>original: *Receptackel*</note> and place another to receive the oil, which is then the Fire. Thus they have separated three elements from one another—namely Water, Air, and Fire—but the earthly substance remains at the bottom as a burnt matter, and

# Philippus Ulstadius. XVIII #

...the / and each of these elements they keep separately in their own vessels.

But if you wish to *rectify* <term>rectification; the process of purifying a liquid by repeated distillation to reach its highest potency</term> one of the elements (if that is necessary, or if you wish to use it otherwise for medicine), then distill that same water seven times through an *alembic* <term>alembic; a cap or hood used in distillation to collect and cool vapors</term>, and for each distillation, wash the *feces* <note>original: *Feces*; referring here to the solid dregs or sediment remaining after distillation</note> cleanly out of the *cucurbit* <term>cucurbit; the gourd-shaped bottom part of a still containing the liquid to be distilled</term>, which should be mixed with the earthly element and distilled in a *Balneo Mariæ* <term>Bain-marie; a gentle water bath used to heat substances evenly</term>. In this same way, you shall also rectify the Air through a sevenfold repetition of the aforementioned work, always anew in the ashes <note>original: *Eschen*; likely referring to a sand or ash bath for higher heat</note>, mixing the dregs with the Earth. In this manner, you shall also temper and rectify the element of Fire, and this is the third *Oil* <note>original: *Oleum*</note>. And you shall keep each Oil in a well-sealed vessel.

<margin>For all kinds of medicine.</margin>
Now that the rectifications of each element have been shown, it is proper to know the nature and power of each element as well. Therefore, the watery quality is powerful through its ability to drive out all heat and poison from the chest; if one takes a little of it, it also purifies the blood. It is good against blockages of the lungs and liver; it breaks all *choler* <note>original: *Cholera*; one of the four bodily humors, associated with heat and yellow bile</note>, excess moisture, and *phlegm*. Furthermore, it releases a sick person from all illness in nine days if he takes a little of it every day on an empty stomach and consumes very little food. <margin>Oil of the Air.</margin> The Oil of the Air, however, preserves youth and strength along with beauty and a good form. It does not allow the blood to rot, nor *melancholy* <note>original: *Melancholey*; the humor associated with "black bile" and sadness</note> to rule, nor choler to burn or overheat, nor phlegm to take the upper hand in a person's body. It increases the blood and distributes it to all the limbs; therefore, those who use this Oil should often undergo bloodletting, for it is very penetrating. It sustains and nourishes the sick above all. <margin>For the eyes.</margin> It is also good and helps wonderfully where one stands in worry of losing an eye or their sight, if they put one or two drops into it every day, and continue this for thirty days in a row.

But the power of the fiery quality is much greater than the other two, for it works in matters where the others achieve nothing. <margin>Maintains youth.</margin> It keeps old people in a youthful state and rejuvenates them again. And even if a person were already at the point of death, or lay in the throes of death, and took or used as much of this fiery oil as a grain of wheat, tempered with wine or with an *Aqua Vitæ* <term>Aqua Vitæ; "Water of Life," a concentrated medicinal alcohol or distilled spirit</term>, <margin>Life.</margin> it raises him up again to life. <margin>For the heart.</margin> It warms the blood, gladdens the heart, protects a person for a long time from death, and brings back to him the power of his youth. And if one uses it with rye water, it is held to be an *Elixir* or *Medicine of Life*. The earthly matter, rectified three times through *dissolution*, *coagulation*, and *calcination* <note>original: *Dissolution, Coagulation, vnd Calcination*; the alchemical processes of dissolving, solidifying, and heating to a powder</note>, is a subtle salt of the earth, with which all metals may be turned into a stone, and with which <margin>Spirits fixed</margin> all spirits may be fixed or made stable—those which have a rooted moisture. <margin>For leprosy.</margin> It also nourishes those with leprosy. From this, the ancient philosophers made the stone which they called *Lapidis Philosophorum* <note>original: *Lapidis Philosophorum*; the Philosopher's Stone</note>, that is,

### The Fourteenth Chapter: How one should extract the ###
### *Quinta Essentia* from human blood, eggs, ###
### meat, and the like. ###

<margin>How to make the Fifth Essence from human blood.</margin><image-desc>A large ornate decorative drop cap 'W' with scrolled foliage patterns.</image-desc>If you wish to extract the *Quinta Essentia* <term>Quinta Essentia; the "Fifth Essence," the purest and most potent spiritual core of a substance</term> from human blood, then take the blood of a sanguine person, or from a healthy choleric <note>Sanguine and choleric are two of the "four temperaments" in medieval medicine, thought to be the healthiest for this procedure</note> of middle age who drinks good wine. When he has had a vein struck and has been bled, the blood—once it has separated from the water and settled at the bottom of the bleeding bowl—should be ground in a vessel with a tenth part of common salt, well beaten and mixed together. After that, put it into a sealed *pelican* <image-desc>An illustration of an alchemical distillation vessel known as a 'pelican,' featuring two curved side-arms that loop back into the body of the flask.</image-desc> <term>pelican; a specialized vessel designed for "circulatory" distillation, where vapors condense and flow back into the main body</term>. Soon after, set it in a pit five feet deep and two feet wide, in which there shall be a layer of quicklime, then another of horse manure, as was said above. In there, let it ferment for approximately one month, depending on whether the weather is fair or cloudy. Afterwards, when you see the matter dissolved into water, such that the coarse substance has settled at the bottom of the pelican and is completely separated from the water, then take it out of the manure and place it in a *Balneum Mariae* <term>Bain-marie; a gentle water bath used to heat substances slowly and evenly</term> with an *alembic* <term>alembic; the cap or "head" of a still</term>, and distill it with a gentle fire, as was said above concerning the *Quinta Essentia* of wine in the third chapter.

And when the first distillation is completed, mix it again with the *feces* <note>original: *Fecessen*; the solid dregs or residue remaining after distillation</note> which remain in the distilling vessel, and place them back in the manure until the pure is separated from the impure, and you see the subtle matter swimming upon the dregs. When the weather is fair, *putrefy* <note>putrefaction; the process of breaking down a substance through gentle heat and moisture</note> it longer than when it is cloudy, in the place mentioned above. After that, distill it for the second time; soon digest it again with its dregs and distill it once more; this shall be done at least four times. After the fourth distillation, you shall *circulate* <term>circulation; the process of continuous evaporation and condensation to refine a substance to its highest state</term> it for a long time, as was said above regarding the *Quinta Essentia* of wine, until it reaches perfection and becomes indestructible. Thus through distillation, the *Quinta Essentia* from the blood of the human body is prepared. This *Quinta Essentia* powerfully sustains human nature in all illnesses, and immediately releases the sick person (whatever the illness may be) from their days of suffering. That shall be enough concerning human blood.

As for meat, from which you wish to extract the *Quinta Essentia*, you shall cut it subtly and small, and then pound it in a mortar with a tenth part of common salt.

Philippus Ulstadius. XIX

The same should be done with eggs in a mortar, until they turn to water; thereafter, put them into a cucurbit <term>cucurbit; a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</term> and place upon it a blind alembic—that is, a still-head without a spout <note>original: *vngeschnebelten Helm*; a "beakless" cap used when the goal is to circulate vapors back into the vessel rather than collecting them elsewhere</note>. Otherwise, all other steps should be carried out just as described for human blood.

### The Fifteenth Chapter: How one should extract the ###
### *Quinta Essentia* from apples, pears, ###
### and other fruits. ###

<image-desc>Decorative historiated initial S featuring intricate scrolling vine work and foliage motifs.</image-desc>
If you wish to extract the *Quinta Essentia* <term>Quinta Essentia; the "Fifth Essence," the purest medicinal core of a substance</term> from apples, pears, plums, cherries, quinces, and all kinds of fruit, then cut them as small as possible with a knife; thereafter, pound them in a stone mortar and mix them very well with a tenth part of common salt. Then put them into a cucurbit and place a blind alembic upon it; seal it with luting <note>original: *verlutier*; using a paste or clay to make the vessel airtight</note> as best as possible, and place it in manure to putrefy, as was previously described regarding human blood. And then it is to be observed whether any of these fruits possess the virtue or power for which you desire to use them, for the power and virtue of the same fruit comes from its essence, which is secret and hidden within the matter. And once this essence is separated from its dregs <note>original: *secessen*; waste or sediment</note> and coarse matter, and subsequently brought into a state of immateriality and near-indestructibility, it is then not without reason called the *Quinta Essentia* of that same fruit by the philosophers. And for that reason, it has a thousand times greater power than before, when it was still an elemental body. For the dregs of the fruits (as no one doubts) are composed or put together from the four qualities <note>The four qualities are hot, cold, moist, and dry, which make up the physical elements</note>.

### The Sixteenth Chapter: How one should extract the ###
### *Quinta Essentia* from flowers, herbs, ###
### and roots. ###

<image-desc>Decorative historiated initial B with ornate leaf patterns and stylistic floral elements inside the loops.</image-desc>
If you desire to extract the *Quinta Essentia* from the four qualities of flowers, herbs, and roots, then take them when they are well-ripened, with their entire substance, when the weather is clear and beautiful, during the waxing of the moon and when it is nearly full. For at that time, all flowers, herbs, and roots are taken at a point furthest from decay; thus, they should be drawn away from all foreign and supernatural heat, and be quite well cleansed of all impurity. Thereafter, cut them as small as possible and pound them in a marble mortar with a tenth part of salt, and put them into a circulatory vessel <term>circulatorium; a specialized glass vessel designed to let vapors rise, condense, and flow back down in a continuous cycle</term>. Ferment them in horse manure for a whole month, and renew the manure once every week. At the end of the month, take them out of the manure, remove the blind helm <note>The beakless still-head</note>, and place a spouted one upon it; and distill it in a water bath <term>Balneo Marie; "Mary’s Bath," a vessel of hot water used to heat the glass gently and evenly</term>. Increase the first fire until the third degree <note>Medieval alchemists used four degrees of heat, with the third being quite strong, often a boiling heat</note>, and keep the distilled water.

# Of the Secrets of Nature #

Digest it again as before, and distill it for a third time, always <term>putrefying</term> <note>allowing the matter to break down through gentle warmth</note> it, while reducing or easing the fire by half a degree. The time for <term>putrefaction</term> should also always decrease by half; namely, that it occurs in twenty-one days in the second <term>digestion</term>, in fourteen days in the third, and in eight days in the fourth, so that it is fermented four times. <image-desc>A woodcut illustration of an alchemical circulatory vessel, known as a Pelican. It is a round-bottomed flask with two side-arms that loop from the neck back into the body of the vessel, allowing vapors to condense and flow back down for continuous distillation.</image-desc> distillation has occurred, then place it in this <term>circulatorium</term> <note>a circulatory vessel, also called a Pelican</note> and bury it in horse manure or in the water bath, and set the fire to the first degree. For the more it is circulated, the nobler it becomes, as the superfluous moisture of the four qualities is thoroughly digested and consumed, and it dissolves in the circulatory vessel through manifold rising and falling. And thus you have the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: *Quintam Essentiam*</note>, in which the greatest power of the flowers, herbs, and roots is hidden. Yet it is even stronger if you take the herb, seeds, and roots and distill a water from them, then pour the water thus distilled back onto the dregs from which it was distilled, and digest it for seven days in horse manure. Thereafter, distill it through the ashes in every way, form, and manner as you did above with the <term>celandine</term> <note>original: *Schelkraut*; a common herb in alchemical medicine, often used as a standard for these processes</note>, for thereby you will separate the four qualities from them. Thus the matter becomes so much the nobler and better; and keep each thing separate, for otherwise the dregs would be of no use.

But one might ask what the <term>Fifth Essence</term> truly is, since it seems to be nothing more than another distilled water? To this I answer: it is nothing else in form and appearance than another distilled water, but it is much more subtle and noble than an ordinary, simple distilled water. Through much and varied digestion, circulation, and distillation, all phlegmatic and watery moisture within it is consumed. Likewise, through its powerful subtlety, when it is poured onto the dregs and digested again, and distilled again from the dregs, it has recaptured all its strength and power that were hidden within it. And therefore it is compared to burnt wood which has now been burned into charcoal, and yet the charcoal is no longer the flame. It is also compared to a dead man; for when the soul has departed from the body, then the body is of no use but to rot. And for this reason, I have all

## Philippus Ulstadius ##

...thing from the past I wish to reveal, so that if you desire to have something of a subtle nature and great power, you might bring it to effect according to your heart's desire. So, take this <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>Fifth Essence; a highly purified alcohol or "spirit of wine" used as a universal solvent in alchemy</note> instead of other waters, and add it to whatever spices you wish. Distill it in the same manner as you have now often heard; namely, take six ounces of the <term>Quinta Essentia</term> (which is twelve <term>lot</term> <note>a historical German unit of weight, approximately 1/32 of a pound</note>) and three or four parts of other <term>species</term> <note>original: *Species*; here meaning spices or medicinal ingredients</note> (which is one <term>lot</term>). Crush them as finely as possible and put them into a <term>Cucurbita</term> <note>a gourd-shaped glass flask</note>. Set a blind helm <note>an alembic head without a spout, used to keep vapors inside the vessel</note> upon it and digest it in horse manure for a whole month. 

Thereafter, distill it and increase the fire until the end of the third degree <note>alchemical heat was often measured in four "degrees," with the third being a strong, hot fire</note>. After that, crush the <term>feces</term> <note>original: *Feces*; the solid dregs or residue remaining after distillation</note> into powder, pour the water that you distilled from them back onto them, and put it again into a <term>Cucurbita</term>. Set the <term>Alembicum Cecum</term> <note>original: *Alembicum Cecum*; a "blind" alembic or still-head without an exit tube, used for circulation</note> upon it and digest it for three weeks. Thereafter, distill it again and digest it for fourteen days, always making the fire gentler by one degree. 

And when you have distilled and <term>putrefied</term> <note>allowed to rot or break down through gentle warmth</note> it four times, then crush the <term>feces</term> to powder again. Finally, set the fire in the first degree of heat and digest it for eight days; thereafter, put it into a <term>Circulatorium</term> <note>a specialized vessel, like a Pelican, designed for continuous redistillation</note> and circulate it there as long as you wish. Then you will find wonderful effects in every substance for which that same matter is intended. 

But if you believe that an incorruptible thing shall be made from a corruptible thing, then you are deceived, as <term>Johannes de Rupescissa</term> <note>a 14th-century Franciscan alchemist who pioneered the use of "quintessence" in medicine</note> says. For although it makes the corruptible almost equal to the incorruptible, it is nonetheless not used to reach true perfection, as <term>Raymundus Lullius</term> <note>Ramon Llull, a philosopher and mystic to whom many alchemical works were attributed</note> speaks: "For everything that is under heaven is perishable." 

Therefore, it is almost laughable that people prepare and make many kinds of things in hopes that they might protect or preserve various materials entirely from destruction. We see this in the sick, who are sometimes helped by doctors through clumsy means or by chance, even though no rightful <term>Cura</term> <note>original: *Cura*; a proper cure or medical treatment plan</note> or orderly medicine was applied. But yet (as <term>Ovid</term> <note>the Roman poet Ovidius Naso</note> says), "It does not always depend on the doctor whether the sick person recovers." If it were so, then those people would still be alive who died a hundred or two hundred years ago. 

And therefore, even if you used the right and true <term>Aurum potabile</term> <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; "potable gold," a legendary elixir thought to grant near-immortality</note>, as was perhaps in use five hundred years ago, and if you thought because of it to live three, four, or five hundred years (as we read of many others in the past), that is a presumption, and almost to be laughed at in these our times. For the nature of man and his increasing or growing strength decreases from day to day, because no regulation of eating and drinking is kept among people anymore. In our times (as <term>Paul</term> <note>The Apostle Paul</note> also said, stating it was common), there is no one who doubts that more of them die from gluttony than from the sword. In addition, we have no regard for the honoring of God, nor any fear of God or demonstration of service...

<summary>This page provides technical instructions for infusing the Fifth Essence with spices through repeated distillation and circulation, while concluding with a philosophical warning that no medicine, even potable gold, can grant immortality in an age of human decline and gluttony.</summary>

<keywords>Quinta Essentia, distillation, spices, Cucurbita, Johannes de Rupescissa, Raymundus Lullius, Aurum potabile, gluttony vs the sword, human mortality, alchemy</keywords>
