...sealed in a Mary’s Bath <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a water bath or double-boiler used for gentle, steady heating</note> for forty-two days, keeping it in a constant warmth. Afterward, increase the fire to the fourth degree <note>the highest level of heat in traditional alchemy, often compared to a direct flame</note> for one hour, and a gold-colored water will emerge. Keep this water separate, for it is the Potable Gold <term>Aurum potabile; a legendary medicinal liquid made from dissolved gold</term>. 

Afterward, take four pounds of the Quintessence <term>Quinta Essentia; a highly distilled alcohol or "spirit of wine" believed to hold the life-force of a substance</term> and add to it fine gold, pearls, ambergris, musk, amomum, anise, the larger variety of cardamom, ginger, cinnamon, zedoary <note>a root similar to ginger</note>, mace, nutmeg, cloves, Oriental saffron, cubebs, agarwood <note>original: *Paradiß holz*</note>, *Turbith Mundi* <note>a purgative root used in medieval medicine</note>, <margin>The Latin copy has one dram <note>original: *quintlin*</note> of senna leaves.</margin> choice white resins, well-gummed white agaric <note>a medicinal fungus</note>, five myrobalans <note>dried medicinal fruits</note>, senna leaves—one *lot* <note>a weight of approximately half an ounce</note> of each—half a *lot* of rhubarb <note>original: *Rauenfentij*</note>, and six *lot* of lemon-colored aloe juice.

Crush all these things together and place them for a whole <margin>To preserve health.</margin> month in a Mary's Bath. And when you wish to keep someone in their health, you should give them one dram <note>original: *quintlin*</note> of the first distilled water in a drink. But to release an unhealthy or <margin>To help the sick.</margin> sick person from their illness, you should give them one dram of the first water and two drams of the second water. However, if the illness is very severe, you might give two drams of the first water and one of the second. And in this recipe lies the secret of all secrets for sustaining human nature until the goal set for us by God. 

Therefore, Avicenna <note>a famous 11th-century Persian physician and philosopher</note> says: Take black knapweed <note>original: *Jacea nigra*</note>, crush it in a mortar, and distill the juice you squeeze out of it through an alembic <term>a distillation cap or apparatus</term> in a Mary's Bath. Do the same with bugloss <note>original: *Buglossa*</note>, called "Oxtongue" in German. Afterward, take half an ounce of each water and three grains of fine, thin gold leaf. The patient should drink this on an empty stomach for three days in a row, though he should first purge the body.

<margin>For leprosy</margin> This aforementioned gold also helps against leprosy if one drinks every day one scruple <note>a small weight, roughly 1.3 grams</note> of the first and two scruples of the second, with half an ounce of water distilled from an old capon <note>a castrated rooster, often used in restorative broths</note>. 

But Raymond Lully <note>a 13th-century philosopher and alchemist</note> says one should drink it with a certain julep or syrup: Take wild syrup <note>original: *Syropi agreste*</note> and simple vinegar syrup <note>original: *Syropi acetosi Simplicis*</note>, one *lot* of each; two *lot* of oxtongue water; and four *lot* of chicory water <note>original: *aque Dicoree*; also called "Sun-whirl" or "Way-wise" in German</note>. Make a julep and give it to the leper with the aforementioned gold. 

And John of Rupescissa <note>a 14th-century alchemist known for his work on the Quintessence</note> says: This Potable Gold, taken with four *lot* of celandine water, helps greatly against all illnesses, and most of all against consumption <note>wasting diseases like tuberculosis</note> and dropsical fever. 

Also know that a youth who wishes to use this gold should be bled at least twice a year. But an old person may take one drop every day with five drops of Quintessence on an empty stomach and should fast for a whole hour afterward; for it wonderfully drives away gray hair or grayness. <margin>Strengthening the heart</margin> It also strengthens the heart, brain, and all other noble organs.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for preparing alchemical medicines, specifically focusing on a "true" Potable Gold recipe that combines distilled herbal waters with the essence of gold and pearls. It follows the previous discussion on the Quintessence and precious spices.</meta>

### Philippus Ulstadius.

We had intended to write nothing further regarding <term>Potable Gold</term> <note>original: *Auro potabili*; a legendary medicinal liquid made of gold, believed to be a universal cure</note>. However, the true love I hold for all diligent students—whom I know are eager to read better and more useful things than I have written until now—will not permit such a silence. Therefore, so that we do not perhaps make them weary or unwilling with too much difficulty, we will now describe how to make the right, true Potable Gold. This is a medicine that no philosopher could ever find fault with, no matter how experienced or skilled he may be; for he cannot prove that it is harmful or contrary to human nature, seeing as one may give it to a person in the very greatest distress of their illness. It is praised and exalted to the highest degree by Arnoldus de Villanova <note>Arnaldus de Villa Nova (c. 1240–1311), a famous physician and alchemist</note> and the aforementioned Marsilius Ficinus <note>Marsilio Ficino (1433–1499), an influential Italian scholar and priest who wrote extensively on health and longevity</note>. And although it does not require great expense, it is nonetheless not to be despised for that reason. It is made as follows:

Take <term>Bugloss</term> <note>original: *Buglossam*; also known as Ox-tongue herb</note>, with its flowers, roots, and its whole substance. Gather it on a beautiful, clear day, provided it has not rained during the previous night, when the moon is waxing and gazes upon Jupiter and Venus with a friendly aspect <note>This refers to astrological timing; a "friendly aspect" (like a trine or sextile) was believed to imbue the plants with celestial healing power</note>. Crush it with all its substance and digest it in horse manure <note>used as a source of gentle, steady heat</note> for eight days. Distill it three or four times, and each time pour the distilled matter back onto its dregs <note>original: *Feces*</note>. Afterward, digest it again for four or five days in a <term>Mary’s Bath</term> <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a water bath or double-boiler used for gentle heating</note>.

In the same way, you should take <term>Borage</term> <note>original: *Boraginem*</note>, the herb, with its whole substance, at such a time and moonlight as was said before regarding the Bugloss. Always rub its dregs very fine after each distillation and pour the distilled matter back upon them. And you should do exactly the same with <term>Celandine</term> <note>original: *Chelidonia*; also known as swallow-wort</note> and <term>Lemon Balm</term> <note>original: *Melissa*; also called motherwort, heart-wort, or honey-flower</note>.

And when you have these distilled waters in the manner described, mix them together in equal parts—as much of one as of the others—and put this into a glass with a long neck and place it in the sun. You should keep these waters very well until you wish to use them. 

Therefore, when you wish to make a Potable Gold, take one pound of these mixed waters and put them into an earthenware vessel that is glazed on the inside. Take a leaf of fine gold that is not counterfeit, weighing 32 *lot* <note>a weight of approximately half an ounce per *lot*</note>, or as much as you are able to have, for the more, the better. You shall heat that same plate or gold leaf until it glows red, and then quench it in these waters, repeating this until some of the gold is consumed; that is the opinion of Marsilio Ficino. Afterward, strain it very well through a clean linen cloth and put it into a glass, sealing it as best as possible. 

Then take four drams <note>original: *quintlin*</note> of crushed pearls, and pour upon them 7 *lot* of <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: *Aqua Vite*; high-proof distilled spirit of wine</note> made from red wine and distilled four times—

<meta>catchword: Distil-</meta>

The process involves distilling several medicinal herbs—Bugloss, Borage, Celandine, and Lemon Balm—under specific astrological conditions before using the resulting water to quench red-hot gold leaf.</summary>

<header>Of the Secrets of Nature</header>

Distill it and put it in a glass, and set it well-sealed in a <term>Mary’s Bath</term> <note>original: *Balneum Marie*; a double boiler or water bath used for gentle, indirect heating</note> for twenty-four hours. When it is distilled, keep it also in its own container. And when you wish to help a sick person whom the doctors have given up for dead, take one <term>lot</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 15-16 grams</note> of the water in which the gold has been quenched, and one <term>quintlin</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 4 grams</note> of the <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: *Aqua vite*; distilled alcohol</note> with the pearls. Mix them together and give it to the sick person every time, and after six hours just as much, and after twelve hours the same amount. However, if it heats the sick person too much, give it to them with one *quintlin* of violet syrup. One could also take one and a half ounces of the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the purified "spirit" or "fifth element" of a substance</note> made from red wine, as we have taught above, into which one should then put one *quintlin* of fine gold in leaves—though not rubbed or made into powder. In this way, the Quintessence draws the color and power of the gold into itself, and these gold leaves shall remain thus in the Quintessence. And when you give the sick person some of the aforementioned waters, add three or four drops of this Quintessence to it.

<margin>To strengthen the heart.</margin>
<term>Marsilio Ficino</term> <note>original: *Marsilius Vicinus*; the Italian Renaissance physician mentioned on the previous page</note> also desires that one should always put gold leaves into the mentioned waters, even though some have already been quenched therein. And you should take, instead of those same gold leaves, the Quintessence which has now drawn the power of the gold into itself. Thus you have the most true <term>Potable Gold</term> <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; a legendary medicinal liquid made of gold</note>, which is better and more precious than if you spent a thousand guilders seeking a more useful or famous one. For it has the power to strengthen the heart and consume all superfluous humors <note>original: *feuchtigkeiten*; referring to the four bodily fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—which were believed to cause illness if out of balance</note>.

<margin>Good blood.</margin>
<margin>Epilepsy.</margin>
It produces very good blood and drives out everything evil. It preserves youth wonderfully and promotes urination. It drives away the <term>falling sickness</term> <note>original: *Fallenden Siechtagen*; epilepsy</note> and insanity, and it also sustains or nourishes lepers. But beware that you do not take the gold of the alchemists for this purpose, for it will cause more harm than benefit (as <term>Arnoldus</term> <note>Arnoldus de Villanova, the medieval physician</note> says) because of the poisonous substances they use therein and mix with it.

### The Twenty-Eighth Chapter: How one shall make a Potable Gold from the Quintessence distilled through the Stone of the Philosophers.

<margin>This Potable Gold expels all sickness.</margin>
To make Potable Gold in another way: Take the Quintessence which is distilled through the aforementioned <term>Stone of the Philosophers</term>, into which put gold leaves and dissolve them in the same Quintessence so that the gold becomes a soft, liquid, drinkable thing. And this has a wonderful effect for the sustaining of life and for expelling all illnesses; it also brings a person who is half-dead back to health...

### I
<meta>Catchword: so fine</meta>

is to be revived and brought back to his former health. And the medicine will be so much more perfect if you add two parts of the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the highly purified "spirit" or concentrated medicinal core of a substance</note> of honey, extracted just as we have taught above. Put it into a <term>Circulating Vessel</term> <note>original: *Circulatorium*; a glass vessel, such as a "pelican," designed so that distilling vapors condense and flow back into the base for continuous refinement</note> so that there is one pound of the aforementioned <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: *Aqua vite*; distilled alcohol used as a solvent</note>, twenty <term>lots</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 15-16 grams</note> of the Quintessence of honey, and four *lots* of gold leaf. Seal the Circulating Vessel at the top in the very best way, and secure it with such wax as we have taught before. Thereafter, set it in a <term>Mary’s Bath</term> <note>original: *Balneum Marie*; a double boiler used for gentle, indirect heat</note> for four months; the more it is circulated, the better the substance becomes, and the more the gold <term>dissolves</term> <note>original: *Soluiert*</note> and incorporates and unites itself with the other materials. But if you cannot obtain gold leaf, take three or four <term>ducats</term> <note>gold coins</note> and beat them as thin as possible, just as the goldsmiths do when they wish to gild something. However, nothing is better than gold leaf, if it can be had from good ducat gold. If you cannot obtain that, and you do not wish to beat the gold in that way, you may turn it into a powder as follows: Take the best gold you can find, or take guilders, and <term>cement</term> <note>original: *Cementier*; a process of refining gold by heating it with reactive salts</note> them thus.

<margin>Cementing.</margin>
Take one part gold, two parts <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: *Spießglaß*; stibnite or antimony sulfide, used in alchemy to "eat" impurities in gold</note>, and a little <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate</note>; melt them together in a crucible until the entire material becomes quite fluid. Thereafter, pour it into a <term>casting mold</term> <note>original: *fusorium*; a conical vessel</note> and stir it constantly. Then let it cool a little, and you will find the gold at the point of the mold, and the antimony will be separated above it. This is the very best way to <term>refine</term> <note>original: *Finiren*</note> the gold. And when all things have cooled, strike the gold in the point away from the other materials; and if it is not fine enough, melt it again with the antimony and do as you did the first time. You may also refine the gold through such a <term>cement</term> as follows.

<margin>Another Cement.</margin>
Take <term>Roman Vitriol</term> <note>original: *Vitriol Romant*; iron or copper sulfate</note>, or in its place <term>reddened saltpeter</term> <note>original: *Salnitri rubificiert*</note>, <term>Sal ammoniac</term> <note>ammonium chloride</note>, <term>Verdigris</term> <note>original: *Spangrün*; the green crust formed on copper</note>, <term>Red Ochre</term> <note>original: *Rötelstein*</note>, and powder of crushed bricks that have been well-fired—take two *lots* of each. Crush all these things into a fine powder and make a dough with <term>urine</term> <note>original: *Vrina*; specifically, "human piss"</note>, and create <term>Layer upon Layer</term> <note>original: *Stratum super Stratum*</note> in a crucible. That is, place a layer of the same dough at the bottom, and another layer of the gold, and so on and so forth until you have placed all the gold inside. And at the very top of the crucible, make a layer of the dough, and upon that another layer of <term>unslaked lime</term> <note>original: *vngeleschtem Kalck*</note>. Know that no piece of gold should touch another. And when you have set everything in this way, make a lid for the crucible that has a hole in the middle made with a needle, and seal the crucible in the very best way with a <term>lute</term> <note>original: *Leymen*; a sealing clay</note> that has been mixed with hair. Thereafter, set a <term>coal fire</term> <note>original: *Kolfewr*</note> a hand’s breadth away from the crucible on all sides for a full hour. When that hour has passed, move the fire closer for another hour. When that is also passed, move it closer still for an hour so that it touches the crucible. Finally, make another fire on top of the crucible so that it touches it everywhere, and that shall also last for an hour. But when the last hour is over, let the crucible cool and break it open.

...so you will find your gold [refined] up to the very highest point, so that nothing remains but a single, pure matter. And in this way, you may refine the gold up to twenty-four <term>degrees</term> <note>original: *Grade*; refers to carats, with 24 being pure gold</note>.

<margin>Royal Cement,</margin>
One may also refine it through the <term>Royal Cement</term> <note>original: *Cement Regal*; a dry refining process using salts to remove impurities like silver or copper from gold</note> for twenty-four hours, thus: take two parts of common prepared salt and one part of the powder of crushed bricks. Make a dough and incorporate it with human urine. Or, if you wish, you may also refine the gold through such a cement: Take 2 <term>lots</term> <note>a historical weight, roughly 15-16 grams</note> of well-prepared common salt, 1 *lot* of brick powder, 2 *lots* of red <term>vitriol</term> <note>iron or copper sulfate</note>, 6 <term>quintlin</term> <note>a small weight, roughly 4 grams</note> of <term>sublimated mercury</term> <note>mercury processed into a crystalline form, usually mercuric chloride</note>. Crush it all small and prepare it like a dough with spring water. Afterward, make *stratum super stratum*—that is, one layer upon the other—
<margin>Another cement.</margin>
as was described in the first cement. After that, pour the gold [into a vessel], and pour over it 1 *quintlin* of <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salis Armoniaci*; ammonium chloride</note> and 1 *quintlin* of sublimated mercury. Add them little by little into the <term>crucible</term> <note>original: *Tiegel*; a heat-resistant melting pot</note> in which the cast gold is.

<margin>Yet another cement.</margin>
There is also yet another cement that is made thus: Take 2 *lots* of Sal Ammoniac, 2 *lots* of <term>verdigris</term> <note>original: *Spangrün*; a green pigment/chemical formed from copper and vinegar</note>, 6 *quintlin* of well-<term>calcined</term> <note>heated to a high temperature to drive off volatile parts</note> vitriol, and make a dry powder; with this, make *stratum super stratum*. And if you refine your gold by this last way, from four parts of gold, no more than three parts will remain to you. But if you desire to separate or refine the gold or silver from the <term>antimony</term> <note>original: *Spießglas*</note>, add a little <term>tartar</term> <note>original: *Weinsteins*; potassium bitartrate</note> to it, so that the antimony cannot reach the gold or silver. But then you should mix your silver with copper.

There is, however, a way to turn the gold into a powder. Take <term>ducats</term> or <term>florins</term> <note>types of high-quality gold coins</note> prepared in the aforementioned way and <term>glow</term> them <note>heat them until they glow red-hot</note>; afterward, beat them as thin as you possibly can, as if you were going to gild something. Then cut them into small strips and add six parts of <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurij*</note> to one part of gold; place each in a separate crucible in a fire until the mercury begins to "smell" <note>This refers to the mercury beginning to vaporize or fume—a dangerous process to do without modern ventilation</note>. Thus heat the gold as well, and pour it onto the smelling mercury, and stir it with a small splinter of wood until it is entirely dissolved; then shake it together again until it cools. Afterward, put it together into a white leather <note>original: *weiß Fell*; likely a chamois or fine sheepskin used as a filter</note>, as the goldsmiths do; bind it tight and squeeze it out so that the mercury passes entirely through the leather. Put the remainder in a crucible in the fire until the mercury smells. Then put it on a <term>marble stone</term> <note>original: *Marmelstein*; used as a flat surface for grinding and mixing</note> and grind it often, and put it back into the crucible until the smoke from the mercury is gone, and grind it again. Do this as often, with warming and rubbing, until the mercury no longer smokes and the gold has become an extremely subtle powder. Then place it in a <term>calcining furnace</term> and make a flaming fire with dry wood—and not with coal—and the fire should last four or five hours. And thus calcine it until you have cleansed the gold from the mercury and see it purified. Then you have prepared your gold in the very best and finest way.

<summary>This page describes various "cementation" methods using salts and acids to refine gold to 24-carat purity, followed by a detailed procedure for creating an amalgam with mercury to reduce the metal into a fine medicinal powder.</summary>

Afterward, wash the same gold three or four times with <term>Aqua vitae</term> <note>literally "Water of Life"; highly concentrated distilled alcohol</note> and mix it thoroughly using a whole quill-pen. Then strain the *Aqua vitae* from it and pour fresh liquid on; do this three or four times. Afterward, dry the same gold over a gentle charcoal fire. Then place it in a <term>Circulatorium</term> <note>a "pelican" or circulation vessel designed for continuous redistillation</note>, as was described above, with the <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>the "Fifth Essence" or most purified spirit</note> of wine. There is another way to make the gold into powder. Have a copper or iron vessel made for you, shaped as you see here. It should
<margin>Powdering gold.</margin>
have a narrow neck which has a cross made of iron wire at the mouth, the width of a <term>ducat</term> or <term>guilder</term> <note>standard gold coins of the era</note>, so that neither of them may fall inside. And it is necessary that the ducat or guilder touches the mouth of the <term>Cucurbita</term> <note>original Latin: "gourd"; a round-bottomed flask with a long neck</note> all around. Heat the same vessel, and at the bottom make a *stratum* or layer of molten lead, one finger thick, and another layer of <term>tartar</term> <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate crystals found in wine barrels</note>. Continue in this manner until the *Cucurbita* is quite full. 
<image-desc>Two woodcut illustrations are positioned on the left side of the upper text block. The top illustration depicts a long-necked flask or cucurbit with a wide base. The bottom illustration shows a cylindrical furnace with flames emerging from its open top and multiple air vents or fuel holes protruding from its sides.</image-desc>
Afterward, lay the ducat or guilder on top of the cross and set it in such a furnace as you see here, so that the lead remains always liquid. And when the gold has been on one side for a while, turn it over to the other side. Thus it becomes prepared to be made into a golden powder. Afterward, <term>calcine</term> <note>to reduce a metal to a powder or "ash" through high heat</note> it in a <term>reverberatory furnace</term> <note>original: *Referber Ofen*; a furnace where heat is reflected or "reverberated" from the roof onto the material</note>, as has just been said. You may do the same with four or five guilders, and also with other silver coins.

## The Twenty-Ninth Chapter: How to make a drinkable gold with honey.

Take well-prepared <term>Sal gemme</term> <note>rock salt or "salt of the gem"</note>, and the very best honey—extracted from the honeycomb without the use of fire, as we have described above—and rectified <term>Aqua vitae</term>, equal parts of each. Distill it all together three times. Afterward, take one pound of this distilled matter and as much gold leaf of the finest quality as you wish, and place it in a *Cucurbita* upon the ashes <note>referring to a sand or ash bath for even heating</note>. Then add <term>selected rhubarb</term> <note>original Latin: *Rheubarbari Electi*</note>, <term>aloeswood</term> <note>original: *Ligni aloes*; also known as "Paradise Wood"</note> well-crushed, <term>Rhapontic</term> <note>original: *Repontici*; a variety of rhubarb used medicinally</note>, the <term>bone from a stag’s heart</term> <note>original: *Ossis de corde cerui*; a rare medicinal substance thought to strengthen the heart</note>, fresh <term>Alexandrian musk</term> <note>original: *Musei Alexandrini*</note>, and good ambergris; one <term>quint</term> <note>a small weight, roughly 4 grams</note> of each. Mix it all well together, and eight days later, filter it through a felt. Thus you have a very good <term>Aurum potabile</term> <note>Latin: "drinkable gold"</note>, useful and healing for many illnesses, which wonderfully restores or refreshes the human body from whatever great sickness it may have.

# How one makes a Drinkable Gold <note>original Latin: *Aurum potabile*</note> solely from gold and "water of life" <note>original: *Aqua vite*; a term for highly concentrated distilled alcohol</note>.
### The Thirtieth Chapter.

Take four <term>quintlin</term> <note>a unit of weight, approximately 4 grams</note> of fine gold, beat it until it is as thin as possible, and cut it into small pieces. After that, take two <term>lot</term> <note>a weight equal to about 15 grams</note> of purified mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> and make an <term>amalgam</term> <note>a soft mixture of mercury and another metal</note>. Place it upon a marble stone, add live sulfur <note>original: *Schwefel*</note> to it, and rub them well together. Afterward, place it in a crucible <note>original: *Tiegel*</note> over a charcoal fire and let the sulfur burn away completely. Then place it in a <term>cupellation furnace</term> <note>original: *Cinericium*; a furnace used for refining precious metals using ashes</note>. After that, place it again in a crucible over a fire until the crucible begins to glow red. Then draw the crucible out, and you will find your gold yellow and powdered most finely. Following this, place it in a <term>reverberatory furnace</term> <note>original: *Reuerberofen*; a furnace that reflects heat onto the substance</note>; in this way, your gold will be prepared in the best possible manner. Afterward, wash it with a single, simple "water of life" <note>distilled alcohol</note> and dry it. Take as much of this powder as you wish and pour as much rectified <term>Aqua vitae</term> <note>alcohol distilled multiple times to increase its potency</note> onto it so that it rises three fingers' width above the gold. Put it then into a <term>circulation vessel</term> <note>original: *Circulatorium*; a "pelican" flask designed for continuous redistillation</note> and place it in a water bath <note>original: *Balneum Marie*; literally "Bath of Mary," a gentle heating method</note> with its small stones <note>likely boiling chips or specific minerals used to regulate temperature</note> at half the weight of the gold, and circulate it as is proper. Thus the gold dissolves and becomes a yellow water, like saffron, so that it even tints human nature <note>likely meaning it affects the complexion or bodily humors</note>. And thus you have a true "drinkable gold."

# The Thirty-First Chapter / Concerning a Drinkable Gold of a Bishop of Trier.

A highly venerable Bishop of Trier makes a drinkable gold <note>original: *Aurum potabile*</note> in this way: When you have distilled the "water of life" <note>distilled alcohol</note> and the <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: *Flegma*; the watery, less volatile byproduct of distillation</note> settles like a tough matter at the bottom of the still <note>original: *Distillatory*</note>, take the *Aqua vitae* that has been distilled twice in a water bath and pour it onto the black matter once it has cooled. Let it stand thus for a day and a night. Afterward, draw the *Aqua vitae* away from it again through distillation over a gentle fire until you reach that same matter. Warm it gently if you wish to keep the glass vessel intact. Then pour spring water upon that same black matter to a height of six fingers and let it rest thus for three or four days. After that, wash it with other spring water, and you will find the substance or matter at the bottom, looking like sand; this is the "earth," which you should dry out. When it is dry, pour rectified *Aqua vitae* onto it to a height of four fingers, and let it remain thus in a water bath...

...remain for twenty-three hours. Seal the mouth of the glass with another glass, and seal it with such wax as was described above. Afterward, draw the "water of life" <note>original Latin: *Aquam vite*; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> back down. You shall do this often; the more often you pour it on and draw it back off, the better it is. And when the "earth" <note>the solid residue remaining after distillation</note> has dried out, then bury the <term>cucurbit</term> <note>a gourd-shaped glass boiling flask</note> up to its neck in sand and draw it off through a strong fire until you see no more smoke or vapor in the <term>alembic</term> <note>the distillation lid or head that fits onto the cucurbit</note>. Do not remove the receiver vessel from there, for it is necessary that the water of life and the spirit—or that same vapor—mix with one another.
<margin>Fire and Air.</margin>
And thus you have two elements, namely Fire and Air. After that, grind the earth very well upon a marble stone and put it into a cucurbit; pour a rectified water three or four fingers high upon it, and place it in a <term>Mary’s bath</term> <note>original: *Balneũ Marie*; a water bath used for gentle, even heating</note> for a day and a night until it dissolves; and when it is dissolved, let it cool. After that, draw it off again and let the earth dry in the sun. Once dried, you shall put it into a crucible and seal it in the very best way with the <term>Lute of Wisdom</term> <note>original: *Luto Sapientie*; a specialized airtight sealant made of clay, lime, and other materials</note>, and <term>calcine</term> <note>heat to a high temperature to reduce to a powder</note> it in a <term>reverberatory furnace</term> <note>a furnace where the heat is reflected or "reverberated" off the roof onto the material</note> for four hours. After that, let it cool and grind it again upon the marble stone; put it into a cucurbit and pour rectified "strong water" <note>original: *Aqua fort*</note> upon it, and place it in a Mary’s bath to dissolve. After that, draw the water of life away from it again and calcine the earth again for four hours until it becomes dry. Or, one may also burn it so that it glows red; afterward, dissolve it again in a Mary’s bath with water of life. And dissolve it and calcine it as often as until you can no longer calcine or dissolve it; and this usually happens in seven repetitions, if the master does it correctly. But if you wish to recognize when the earth will let itself be dissolved no further, then take a little of that same earth and dry it in the air, and pour three or four drops of fresh water upon it. If it dissolves or disappears, it is a sign that it is not yet sufficiently prepared. And for that reason, you must dissolve it again in a Mary’s bath and afterward calcine it until you have a true indication that it can no longer be dissolved. At the last, however, you shall dry it and calcine it and let it cool; then it is for itself alone, and Fire and Water have been separated from it. Afterward, you shall place all these waters, with which you dissolved the aforementioned earth, together with the earth into a cucurbit in a Mary’s bath, and add the "Fifth Essence" <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the purest, most medicinal part of a substance</note> to it. And draw off half of it with a gentle fire; afterward, set another receiver vessel before it and strengthen the fire until all moisture and "phlegmatic" <note>watery or dull</note> water separates from it. When that has happened, then strengthen the fire even more to drive out the spirits, until you see that the small leaflets <note>crystalline flakes or patterns</note> appearing in the alembic have completely vanished. After that, calcine it and dissolve it again as you did above; this shall happen seven times, and just as often one shall dissolve and calcine the earth.
And afterward, grind it again upon the marble stone and place it in a <term>pelican</term> <note>a specialized circulating vessel with two "arms" that allow condensed vapors to flow back into the body</note> to circulate in the ashes, and also to...

# Of the Secrets of Nature #

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration of a Pelican alchemical flask, a vessel with two side-tubes that loop back into the body, used for continuous distillation/circulation.</image-desc> Dissolve for eight days; then you will see the matter <term>coagulate</term> <note>turn from a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state</note>. However, if you do not have a <term>Pelican</term> <note>a specialized glass vessel designed for "circular" distillation, where vapors rise and flow back into the base via side arms</note>, you should put it in a circulating vessel. And when you see that the matter is coagulated, you shall dissolve it again in a <term>Mary’s bath</term> <note>original: *Balneo Marie*; a gentle water bath or bain-marie</note>, and afterward coagulate it again in the ashes <note>an ash bath provides a higher, dryer heat than a water bath</note>. And do this so often until a substance comes out like an oil, and that is the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual concentration of a substance</note>. But if you perhaps have neither a Pelican nor a circulating vessel:

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration of two cucurbits (flasks) joined mouth-to-mouth, used as a circulatory vessel.</image-desc> Then take two <term>cucurbits</term> <note>gourd-shaped glass boiling flasks</note> of the same size and invert them upon one another, so that they are both well-leveled and matched at the mouth as best as possible. Into these, you shall put the <term>calcined earth</term> <note>the powdery residue left after burning a substance to ash</note> and mix just as much "foreign water" with it until it becomes as thick as mustard. And there should be an iron ring around the union of the two so that they can be joined together and sealed with the aforementioned wax; it will thus become like a <term>blind alembic</term> <note>original: *Alembico Ceco*; a distillation head with no spout, forcing all steam to condense and fall back down</note>. After this, set it to circulate in a Mary’s bath. Afterward, <term>distill</term> it with a good fire through an <term>alembic</term> <note>the cap or head of a still</note> until the dry matter remains at the bottom, and keep the water that has been distilled from it. Afterward, pour the other water onto the matter which you have drawn off from the first circulation, and circulate it for eight days; distill it again, and do this always every eight days until all the waters are "imbibed" <note>original: *eingedrenckt*; soaked into the dry material until the solid can hold no more liquid</note>. And when that is done, put all the waters together into a vessel and pour a rectified <term>water of life</term> <note>original: *Aqua vite*; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> onto the matter, and <term>calcinate</term> <note>burn to a high heat</note> it for four hours in a calcining furnace until it becomes beautifully white. Weigh it every time so that you can notice how much it increases. After that, put it back with its waters into a circulating vessel with a blind alembic on top, also sealed as before, and set it in the bath to circulate for two months or longer, depending on how much matter there is. Afterward, take the alembic down, and you will find the matter beautiful, translucent, and clear as a crystal, in the size of pearls at the bottom of the circulating vessel; and that is the true, genuine Quintessence. And therefore, if you wish to make a proper <margin>To make a true Drinkable Gold.</margin> and true <term>drinkable gold</term> <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; a legendary medicinal panacea</note> with it, you should mix one <term>quintlin</term> <note>a small unit of weight, roughly 4 grams</note> of the finest gold with three <term>lots</term> <note>a unit of weight, roughly 15-16 grams</note> of Quintessence, and pour upon it two and a half pounds of the very best <term>composite water of life</term> <note>original: *Aqua vite Composite*; distilled alcohol infused with spices or herbs</note> that you can obtain. You may also add twelve parts of the Quintessence of honey to it, and you shall seal the glass very well and set it in a Mary’s bath; then you will see the wonderful effects of the gold and the Quintessence, for they always rise and fall together until they both become a thick oil like human blood. And when you see the oil thus coagulated, let the glass cool, then seal it very well and place it in a cellar; so it will dissolve by itself. Afterward, coagulate it again as before, and dissolve it thus until it no longer allows itself to be coagulated—

Philippus Ulstadius. XXXXVI

...powdering, nor by a great fire; for know that the matter is sufficiently prepared.

<margin>This oil has great virtue and protects the healthy.</margin>
This Oil is the very best medicine for all grave illnesses, and also protects healthy people from all diseases. Afterward, take care that you give it neither to the healthy nor the sick unless it is necessary; that is, you should barely give them three drops in three months. If you give it to them often, the soul would so delight or rejoice therein <note>The author suggests the medicine is so potent or spiritually "pleasurable" to the vital spirits that frequent use would be overwhelming.</note> that it would be far too much. In the summer, give it with pure well water, but in winter, with the broth of a boiled <term>capon</term> <note>a castrated rooster, traditionally used for making restorative broths</note>, or with wine.

If you wish to make the little stones or crystals by a shorter path, then distill the <term>phlegm</term> <note>the watery, tasteless byproduct of distillation</note> that remained after the first distillation of the water. Put it into a <term>cucurbit</term> <note>a gourd-shaped glass boiling flask</note> and distill it through sand <note>using a sand-bath for even heating</note> until the black matter remains at the bottom, thin like a coarse, thick wine. This you shall put into another cucurbit and pour phlegm over it again, and distill as at first. And do the same again so that you may extract the matter; this should be put into the kettle and left there until it is turned back into a thin substance, as it was in the beginning. Then place it again in a cellar and cover it well so that no uncleanness falls into it, and leave it there to cool for a month or longer. Afterward, pour pure well water upon it and mix it together with your hand, and strain it off into a glazed jar; thus you will find small, transparent little stones like a crystal. And that is the true <term>Earth</term> <note>in alchemy, "Earth" refers to the solid, fixed salt or residue of a substance</note>, which one should grind and mix with the gold that is in the <term>circulatory vessel</term> <note>a vessel designed for "circulation," where vapors condense and flow back into the base</note>, with the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual extract</note> of wine and honey; thus you will see the dissolution of the gold. Take out the remainder and make an oil from it, as is shown above; and through this path, you will also have a good <term>potable gold</term> <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; gold dissolved into liquid form, considered the ultimate medicine</note>.

## The Thirty-Second Chapter / How to make a Potable Gold with Greek wine, or Malmsey, or similar wines, and with many kinds of good, precious spices.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'D' featuring floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
You should take the best wine you can have, such as <term>Malmsey</term> <note>a sweet, high-alcohol wine from Greece</note>, <term>Bastard</term> <note>a sweetened, often spiced Spanish wine</note>, Roman, German, Greek, or Spanish wine, and distill it through an <term>alembic</term> <note>the cap or "head" of a still</note> until it may be <term>rectified</term> <note>purified and strengthened through repeated distillation</note>...

<meta>This page continues the instructions for refining alchemical substances. The previous page ended with the description of a potent oil and the preparation of matter in a kettle. # On the Secrets of Nature

...be fixed, as was said above regarding the *Aqua vitæ* <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"; literally "water of life," a term used for highly concentrated alcohol or ethanol</note>. And when you have a good portion of it thus distilled, you shall distill it again six or seven times. Afterward, put it to circulate in a *pelican* <term>pelican</term> <note>a specialized alchemical vessel with two side-arms that feed back into the body, allowing for continuous circulation and reflux of the liquid</note>. Fill it a third or nearly halfway, and seal the opening well with a paste made of egg whites and the best or purest flour. Set it in a *bain-marie* <term>Balneum Mariæ</term> <note>a water bath used to provide gentle, consistent heat</note> over a gentle fire for forty days or longer. Afterward, test it; if you taste or find a sweetness or a very pleasant scent within it, then consider it sufficiently circulated. If not, you shall put it back into the pelican and proceed as before until you achieve this result. And when it reaches that state, put it into a glass and seal it very well; that is the *Quinta Essentia* <term>Fifth Essence</term> of wine, which should be taken before all other medicines. It should be given to the sick early in the morning. The dose is from 2 to 3 *quintlin* <note>a "quintlein" was a historical unit of weight, roughly 3.6 to 4 grams</note>, depending on whether the illness is great or small. 

If you wish to add or mix spices with it, you may take nutmeg, cinnamon sticks, cloves, zedoary, galangal, white ginger, and grains of paradise—two *lot* <note>a "lot" was a weight unit, approximately 15-18 grams</note> of each. Grind them into a fine powder and add 3 *lot* of this spice mixture to one pound of the aforementioned water. Put it in a glass with a long neck, seal it well, and place it on the ashes for twenty hours; shake the glass three or four times. Afterward, strain it through a linen cloth and press the *feces* <note>original: "Feces"; in alchemy, this refers to the solid residue or dregs left after a process, not biological waste</note> out of it. The other material you shall distill as many times as possible, until it can be distilled no further. Afterward, you shall circulate it as before; and thus you have the Fifth Essence and a true *Aurum potabile* <term>Potable Gold</term> <note>literally "drinkable gold," a legendary alchemical remedy believed to cure all ailments and restore youth</note>.

## The Thirty-Third Chapter / Regarding
## another Potable Gold.

Some distill *Aqua vitæ* and extract it from the very best wine they can obtain, in such a way that the *phlegmatic* matter <note>the watery, inactive, and tasteless byproduct of distillation</note> remains at the bottom. Therefore, so that you are not misled or deceived in this, you should first distill half of the volume; the other half is like pure spring water. Do not let this surprise you; you shall distill this again with a gentle fire, then keep it separately. But the thick matter that remains at the bottom, boil it down until it becomes dry. You may also extract a thick black oil from it, which is hot in nature; you should also keep this very well.

Afterward, you shall *calcine* <term>calcine</term> <note>to heat a substance to high temperatures in a furnace to reduce it to a stable powder or ash</note> the black matter in the calcining furnace. Philippus Ulstadius. XXXVII

...you will find a white earth. Therefore, see that you act wisely, so that you may obtain a good portion of that same earth, which you can make clean and white through the heat of fire and the moisture of water.

And thus you may notice that the water is useful for nothing else than for cleansing; therefore, it should have no *Spiritus* <note>original: "Spiritum"; refers to the volatile, active essence of a substance</note> or spirit. It is indeed true that in the *Aqua vitæ* <note>original: "Aqua vite"; "water of life" or concentrated alcohol</note> there is fire and spirit, but it nevertheless has the quality of water within it, which is the *phlegm* <term>flegma</term> <note>in alchemy, the watery, tasteless, and inactive residue left after distillation</note> and the earth, which are obstacles to perfection. And for that reason, one should distill it six or seven times so that it is well rectified; afterward, one should circulate it in the *Quinta Essentia* <term>Fifth Essence</term>. And if this *Aqua vitæ*—that is, the spirit and the fire—did not have the nature of the Fifth Essence from the aforementioned earth, it could not (according to the opinion of Hermes <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy</note>) rise upward, nor could the spirit and the fire be fixed into the earth. 

However, this is the *Aqua vitæ* of the Fifth Essence: you shall have a *cucurbit* <term>Cucurbita</term> <note>a gourd-shaped glass vessel</note> with a beaked *alembic* <term>Alembico</term> <note>a still-head or cap for the vessel with a downward-pointing tube</note> or helm, and put into it four pounds of rectified *Aqua vitæ*. Seal the alembic very well with a dough or paste, as has often been said above, and set it in a furnace to distill, as has often been shown. Take the distilled water into the receiver once it has settled and clarified, and keep it well-stopped in the glass so that no air can reach it, for it is nothing other than a spirit; then extinguish the fire. Do this three times, and each time leave something at the bottom of the *distillatory* <note>the distilling vessel</note> so that you are certain it is nothing but a well-rectified spirit. And you shall distill it thus four times, and you will have a water of a very sweet scent and of wonderful 

<margin>Expelling poisonous air.</margin>
effects. If such a poison or poisoned smoke were to come into a house, it will be wonderfully driven out from there. And 

<margin>Dissolves gold and precious stones.</margin>
before you distill it three times, you will perceive a wonderfully good scent. And this water dissolves gold and all precious stones.

To make this *Aurum potabile* <note>original: "Aurum potabile"; literally "drinkable gold," a legendary alchemical medicine believed to grant longevity</note>, some take gold leaves of the very finest quality, rubbed on a marble stone with honey until they become like water, or until they appear thin and subtle enough to write with. Afterward, wash it in a clean vessel with warm, clear spring water, and let it settle to the bottom. Then put it into an *Aqua vitæ* or into a *Quinta Essentia*, and you will have a very good *Aurum potabile*. And this *Aurum potabile* wonderfully takes away all illnesses, and 

<margin>Leprosy.</margin>
especially leprosy <note>original: "Auſſatz"; in the 16th century, this term could refer to various chronic skin diseases or true leprosy</note>.

# Of the Secrets of Nature /

If you wish to create a Potable Gold <note>original: "Aurum potabile"; a legendary medicinal cordial made of dissolved gold</note> with precious stones—namely, emeralds, sapphires, rubies, garnets, hyacinths, or even with pearls—it is shown here. You must grind them all small upon a marble stone and put that same powder into the aforementioned Water of Life <note>original: "Aqua vite"; likely a highly rectified alcohol used as a solvent</note> to circulate for a time in a Pelican <note>an alchemical vessel with "arms" that allow condensed vapors to flow back into the main body, facilitating constant circulation</note> and thereafter distill it. This water dissolves all things and rises upward, just as if it were flying. And when you make it rise over the gold, it shall henceforth no longer rise upward, and that is the most hidden secret in nature. Now we shall proceed, so that we might make the aforementioned white earth rise upward, so that it may gain the nature of the Fifth Essence <note>original: "Quinta Essentia"; the purest, most potent medicinal core of a substance</note> to dissolve gold or silver. Therefore, take of the white matter, gold, and silver, an equal amount of each, and just as much by weight of the aforementioned Water of Life.

When the gold is dissolved <note>original: "Soluiert"</note>, put it into a cucurbit <term>Cucurbita</term> <note>a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</note> that is not too high, for otherwise it might not be able to rise, and make it rise through an alembic <term>Alembicum</term> <note>a still-head or cap with a downward-pointing beak</note>. And when it rises, then proceed further as is proper. But if you do not have a suitable cucurbit at hand, then take a retort <note>original: "Retorta"</note> in the form that you see here.

<image-desc>Woodcut illustration of an alchemical retort (Retorta), a glass distillation vessel with a bulbous body and a long, downward-pointing neck.</image-desc>

It is also to be known that when the Water of Life is cleansed and rectified, and brought to a Fifth Essence with a most lovely scent, it has its own "earth" that comes from it, which enters the water and rises upward as if it were flying through the nature of the water, which makes the earth rise with it. Therefore, if it happens that you understand these things said here, you may make a water to dissolve all metals. Therefore, take great care that you understand it, or you may achieve nothing, for in this lies the way of all dissolution. Regarding the medicine after the Projection <note>the final stage of alchemical transformation where the medicine is ready to be used</note>, which you shall give to a person, you should take a portion the size of a grain of barley and warm it well in a silver beaker with white wine or pure water until it dissolves. Thereafter, take it from the fire and stir or beat it with a silver spoon until it cools. <margin>For leprosy.</margin> To a leper, one should give it on an empty stomach after midnight. One may also give it to any sick person, regardless of what sickness they have; and even if they had lain sick for two months, they shall recover in two days.

A healthy person, however, should use this medicine three times a year. <margin>Arnoldus.</margin> Concerning this, Arnold of Villanova <note>original: "Arnoldus de villa Nova"; a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> says: He cured Duke Henry of Bavaria of leprosy with this, through the intercession of the King of Aragon, the Duke's father; the Duke had been a leper for three months and recovered in three days. He also says that he used this medicine in his old age, and when he, through the frailty of nature,

It seems to those standing around that the dead man is only ecstatic in spirit, and his hair, beard, and nails continue to grow, because the blood in the body cannot rot, being maintained for a long time by the natural heat through the medicine.

## The Thirty-Fifth Chapter / Of a Potable Gold <note>original: "Auro potabili"; a liquid preparation of gold believed to be a universal panacea</note> for Paralysis <note>original: "Pärlin"; refers to palsy or paralysis</note> or

## The Thirty-Sixth Chapter /
### Of another good Potable Gold <note>original: "Auro potabili"</note>.

So take 4 lots <note>a lot is approximately 15-16 grams</note> of the unique Water of Life <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note>; 2 quintlins <note>a quintlin is approximately 4 grams</note> of Stone Borax <note>original: "Boracis de Petra"</note>. Dissolve the borax in the aforementioned Water of Life. Afterward, dissolve 2 lots of sugar candy in 5 lots of Water of Life. Also dissolve 1 lot of camphor <note>original: "Canfer"</note> in 5 lots of Water of Life. Finally, dissolve gold leaf <note>original: "Goldtbletter"</note> of the finest quality in the same Water of Life.

Put these four Waters of Life into a gourd-shaped flask <note>original: "Cucurbitam"</note>; seal it very well and let it rest for one day. Afterward, place a still-head <note>original: "Alembicum"</note> upon it and distill it until half remains. Then place the flask in a damp place, so that the four materials turn into very beautiful and white-glistening crystals. After this, dissolve the following things in the water distilled from these crystals: good ambergris <note>original: "Ambre bone"</note> and fresh fine musk <note>original: "Musci Fini Recentis"</note>, one quintlin of each; and two lots of the best pearls. Place them in a water bath <note>original: "Balneum Mariæ"</note> and seal everything well with putty <note>original: "Verlutier"; to hermetically seal a vessel</note>. In three days, it will become an oil, which is then a Potable Gold for all illnesses.

## The Thirty-Seventh Chapter /
### Of a Potable Gold with *Aqua fortis* <note>original: "Aqua fort"; nitric acid</note>.

Take the very best gold and dissolve it in *Aqua fortis*. Afterward, let it evaporate <note>original: "reuchs ab"; literally "smoke it off"</note> and draw it down through the still-head. However, you should not let it become completely dry, for then it would be like an oil. After that, pour honey-water upon it, which has also been distilled three times through a still-head, and let it rise four fingers high above the material. Place it for four days and four nights to digest <note>original: "Digieren"; a process of gentle heating to extract or chemically change a substance</note> in a water bath. Afterward, draw the honey-water off again through the still-head, and put fresh water in to digest, and do with it exactly as before. Also know that when the honey-water has settled a little, a substance remains there just like an oil. Afterward, draw the honey-water off as often as necessary so that no taste of salt from the substance of the *Aqua fortis* remains therein, in which the gold was dissolved. And if you [find] through the taste of the honey...

XXXIX
Philippus Ulstadius.

...if you sense a bitterness on the tongue, then take a Water of Life <note>original: "Aquam vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> that has been distilled seven times and contains no phlegm <note>original: "Flegma"; the watery, inactive residue of distillation</note>. Dissolve the gold in it, and the water should rise four fingers' breadth above the gold. Afterward, put it into a distillation vessel <note>original: "Distillatorium"</note>, seal it well with good strong lute <note>original: "Luto"; a sealant or paste</note> or lime, and place it in a water bath <note>original: "Balneum Marie"</note> until it is dissolved. However, this may be done more quickly in horse manure <note>horse manure provided a gentle, consistent heat for alchemical digestion</note>, and it is more natural than using a fixation vessel <note>original: "Fixatorium"</note> or a Pelican <note>original: "Pellican"; a circulatory distillation vessel with two side arms</note>. This very substance is the **Potable Gold** <note>original: "Aurum potabile"</note>, which any sick person may take and use in an electuary <note>original: "Latwergen"; a medicinal paste made with honey or syrup</note> or with wine.

The honey water, however, which is poured onto the gold, shall be made thus: Put the honey into a gourd-shaped flask <note>original: "Cucurbitam"</note>, and when it rises up, open the still-head <note>original: "Alembicum"</note> a little so it settles back to the bottom. Do this repeatedly until the honey is completely distilled. For this reason, the first water is white, but the subsequent ones are <margin>For baldness.</margin> good for baldness; for if one rubs a bald head with it, it makes the hair grow. They are also good for the Potable Gold, as they draw all the bitterness out of it.

### Of another Potable Gold.

Next, return it to the fire until it produces a foam, then skim the foam off. Afterward, pour the whites of four well-beaten eggs into the purified honey and let it boil a little, always carefully skimming off the foam. When it has boiled well, strain it through a felt filter <note>original: "filtz"</note>. Then return it to the fire until all the watery parts have evaporated, and then distill it away from the honey. That which you distill from it, distill again three or four times. <image-desc>A woodcut illustration of a gourd-shaped alchemical distillation vessel (an alembic or cucurbit) with a rounded body and a spout-like neck extending to the left.</image-desc>

Afterward, draw the Water of Life down again through a Mary’s bath <note>original: "Balneum Mārie"; a double-boiler or water bath used for gentle heating</note>, and pour six or seven pounds of the cleanest phlegm onto it. Place it in a cold sand bath for six days; you should then wash the substance with the phlegm until it is purified. Then dry it by a very small fire and pour Water of Life over it so that it rises three fingers above it, and draw it down again after four days through the Mary’s bath. You should do this three times. At the last, however, you should place it in a sand bath and draw out the spirits <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> with a strong fire. You will also see an oil <note>original: "Oleum"</note> coming forth; let this pass into the receiver <note>original: "Receptackel"</note> until it stops smelling, for then the earth is sufficiently calcined <note>original: "Calciniert"; burned to a fine ash or powder</note>. This same water should be used thus: take the calcined earth and pour the best Water of Life you can obtain over it. Digest it under a blind alembic <note>original: "Alembico ceco"; a still-head without a spout, used to circulate vapors back into the flask</note> for four days in a Mary’s bath. Afterward, strain it off gradually so that it does not take color from the earth. Following this, pour other Water of Life over it and do as before; do this as often as necessary until the water no longer takes color from the earth. Then place the water with the earth in a Mary’s bath and draw it down with a gentle fire; then you will find a rough white earth like a salt at the bottom of the gourd-shaped vessel <note>original: "Cucurbita"</note>. This you may dissolve <note>original: "Soluieren"</note> and solidify <note>original: "Coagulieren"</note> again, as before, until it seems sharp and refined <note>original: "Subtil"</note> enough to you.

Oil of the Sun <note>original: "Oleum Solis"</note>—that is, the Golden Oil—is made in this way: Take four pounds of prepared Water of Life and seven gold ducats <note>original: "Ducaten"; high-purity gold coins</note>. And when it is dried out, rub one part of the lime with three parts of prepared mastic <note>original: "Mastix"; a resin used as a binder</note>, and add Water of Life to it so that it becomes a dough. Then put it into a glass and set it on fire; thus the wine and the mastic separate from the gold through the burning, and the lime is turned into an oil, which you shall then very neatly remove from it, and

<page-num>XL</page-num>
<header>Philippus Ulstadius. XL</header>

If the calx <note>original: "Kalck"; the powdery residue left after a metal has been heated or "calcined"</note> has not completely dissolved, wash it again with fresh water and process it with mastic <note>a resin used as a binder</note>, doing everything as before. If it has any blackness or impurity, you should wash it with distilled vinegar. When it has turned entirely into an oil, take two parts of the prepared white earth and add it to the mentioned oil along with a little Water of Life <note>original: "Aquam vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> until it becomes a dough. Afterward, set it with the prepared Water of Life to digest in a Mary’s bath <note>original: "Balneum Marie"; a double-boiler or water bath for gentle heating</note> for one month or longer, until you see whether the gold dissolves quickly or slowly.

<margin>Dissolution of the gold.</margin>
the dissolution of the gold is performed thus: Take one lot <term>lot; a historical unit of weight, roughly 15-16 grams or half an ounce</term> of fine gold and six lots of mercury that has been well washed and purified. Create an amalgam <note>a mixture of mercury and another metal</note>. Afterward, separate the mercury from the gold on a cupel <note>original: "Cinericio"; a shallow vessel used in a furnace to separate precious metals from lead or mercury</note>, just as the goldsmiths do; the gold will remain as a yellow flour. Following this, take half an ounce of sal ammoniac <note>original: "Sal armoniac"; ammonium chloride, used as a flux and solvent</note>, put it with the calcined gold into a Water of Life, and set it to digest in a Mary’s bath for six days. Afterward, draw off all moisture through the heat of a fire of the first degree <note>the lowest level of heat in alchemy, similar to a simmer or gentle warmth</note> until it is ready. Put this same prepared gold into a clean glass cucurbit <note>original: "Cucurbit"; a gourd-shaped distillation flask</note> and place a beaked alembic <note>original: "Alembicum"; the lid or "head" of a still with a downward-pointing spout</note> upon it. Seal everything <note>original: "Verlutier"; to seal with "lute," a specialized clay or paste to make the apparatus airtight</note> as best as you can, and pour a little olive oil <note>original: "Baumöls"; literally "tree oil"</note> onto the material. Distill it first with a very gentle fire, but finally with a strong fire, just as one does with *Aqua fortis* <note>original: "Aqua fort"; nitric acid</note>. Afterward, open the cucurbit; if anything remains inside, repeat the process as before and distill it as often as needed until nothing remains in the glass. Then draw off the phlegm <note>original: "Flegma"; the watery, inactive portion of a distillate</note> in the same way through distillation, and you will have a very good Potable Gold <note>original: "Aurum potabile"; a liquid form of gold believed to have universal healing properties</note>, which is proven in many matters.

## The Thirty-Ninth Chapter / Concerning a volatile Potable Gold.

<image-desc>A decorative drop cap 'D' featuring a stylized dragon or serpentine creature entwined within the letter's frame.</image-desc>

The volatile Potable Gold, which is useful and good for many things, is made in this way: Take as much of the best wine as you can obtain and put it into a glass with a long neck. Close it with the Seal of Hermes <note>original: "Sigillo Hermetis"; an airtight, hermetic seal, often made by melting the glass neck shut</note>. Afterward, let it putrefy <note>original: "Putrificier"; a process of slow digestion or rotting meant to break down the substance</note>, guarding it very well so that it does not grow cold. Let it rest until it has settled its dregs or lees, just as vinegar does, and has become yellow. Therefore, let it stand longer until the dregs have completely vanished; it will then have white veins, appearing as if they were tiny fiery flames, which commonly happens within three months. Afterward, put it into a well-closed or sealed cucurbit and distill it over a fire made of dry wood in lukewarm water, as is done in a Mary’s bath. Afterward, take two pounds of this distilled material and put fifteen gold leaves of the finest quality into it. Boil it for twelve hours in a Mary’s bath, and the glass shall be entirely sealed with the Seal of Herme-

## Chapter Forty / Another Potable Gold.

Likewise, take a thick red wine and distill it four times. Afterward, take calcined tartar <note>original: "Weinstein"; the potassium bitartrate crusts found in wine barrels, heated until powdery</note> and place it upon it, and putrefy <note>allow to ferment or break down</note> it for twenty-four hours. Then draw off all moisture with a gentle fire; then you will see that the white tartar has now become black. You shall calcine it again until it becomes as white as snow. Putrefy it again and distill it as before. And in the same way, you shall calcine, digest, and distill it seven times, as before. Afterward, place it in a Mary’s bath <note>original: "Balneum Marie"; a water bath for gentle heating</note>, adding as many of the very best gold leaves as you desire, and pour [the liquid] back on it until the matter is entirely converted into an oil. This is then a very good Potable Gold, which keeps healthy people in their health for a long time.

## Chapter Forty-One / Another Precious Potable Gold

Among the current philosophers, some make a Potable Gold in this manner: Take red or white wine, whichever and however much you wish; distill it through the ashes <note>using a sand or ash bath to distribute heat</note> seven times. Afterward, collect all the dregs <note>original: "Feces"</note>, distill them, and strain the water until it becomes thick like honey. Afterward, pour the phlegm <note>original: "flegma"; the watery, tasteless part of the distillate</note> upon it and let it rest thus for three days and three nights, so that all the little stones <note>crystalline precipitates</note> settle to the bottom. Then wash those same little stones with the phlegm until they are clean, then dry them out and put them into a gourd <note>original: "Cucurbita"; a round-bottomed glass distillation vessel</note> and place a blind alembic <note>original: "Alembicum Cecum"; a still-head without a spout, used to circulate vapors back into the flask</note> upon it. Set it for three days and three nights in a Mary’s bath, pour Water of Life <note>original: "Aquam vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> over it to the height of three fingers, then strain it very gently so that the wine does not become cloudy. And do this until the wine takes no more color from the little stones. Finally, however, draw all the wine away from the stones so that they remain dry in the glass. Afterward, draw off the oil through the sand with a strong fire. And that which remains in the glass after the oil is called a "stinking earth" <note>the chemically exhausted residue</note>, which you should throw away, as it is of no further use. Afterward, place the alembic in the ashes, and you will find the stones as white as snow or salt. And the wine which you have drawn from the stones after they have remained so white, keep for the multiplication—that is, for the in-

# Of the Secrets of Nature

...so that it retains its **virtue** <note>original: "Tugendt"; the inherent healing power or alchemical potency of a substance</note>. And this shall be done subtly in such a way that one knows how to maintain the **Mary’s bath** <note>original: "Balneum"; a gentle water bath</note> with steady moisture, and gradually separate the water from it with a gentle fire; in this way, the substance of the **gold** remains quite dry at the bottom of the distilling vessel. After that, take that same **wine**, and **distill** it through the **alembic** <note>a distillation head</note> as many times as needed until it can no longer be burned, because of the removal and lessening of pure **sulfur** <note>in alchemy, sulfur often represents the principle of combustibility</note>. You should always receive each distillation in its own separate **receiver**. And take from the second water as often as you see nothing or no more [residue] in the alembic. Into that same water, throw the substance of the gold; then it **dissolves** very quickly into a **Vegetable Water** <note>original: "Aquam Vegetabilem"</note>; that is, into a water as mentioned above, which causes the natural moistures and powers to increase and grow, and which sustains and quickens. And this comes about because the **Mercury** <note>the "spirit" or volatile principle</note> is **rectified** <note>purified through repeated distillation</note> by the Mercury from its **phlegm** <note>original: "Flegma"; the watery, inactive part of a substance</note>, until you see that it can no longer be burned. And when that has been done, then mix it with the first substance of the gold; then it is a true **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"</note> or **Potable Gold** <note>original: "Aurum potabile"; gold rendered into a drinkable, medicinal form</note>.

<margin>The first water.</margin>
This, however, is the first water that serves and is useful for the human body. Take **Water of Life** and separate all remaining moistures through distillation from the **Quinta Essentia** <note>the "Fifth Essence" or purest part</note> of the water, which is then its gold; keep that separately. And into the **Vegetable Water**, put three parts of **honey** with its **comb** <note>original: "Weſel"; likely referring to the honeycomb or the crude substance of the hive</note>. Put all of that together into a Mary’s bath to **putrefy** <note>to break down or ferment through gentle heat</note>. This **digestion** should take place nine times in the bath or in horse manure, for by this the water is rectified.

<margin>2</margin>
The **second water** is this: Take a **capon** <note>a castrated rooster, valued in medicine for its "temperate" qualities</note> or an old hen; remove all bones and superfluity from it, and pound the meat small in a stone **mortar**. Afterward, put it in a **cucurbit** <note>a gourd-shaped boiling flask</note> with an alembic, and distill the water from it in a Mary’s bath; keep that also separately.

<margin>3</margin>
The **third water** is this: Take the meat of a hen or capon, distill the **ashes**, and draw all moisture from them.

<margin>4</margin>
Also, see to it that the meat does not burn in the alembic, and keep that water separately as well. The **fourth water** is this: Take three parts of **wine**, put it upon one part of the aforementioned waters, and put it into a cucurbit. Close it very well with its glass lid, **lute** it <note>seal the joints with an airtight paste</note>, and set it in ashes for three days with a gentle fire. Afterward, distill it through the alembic, and keep that same water separately as well.

<margin>5</margin>
The **fifth water** is this: Take the entire substance of a capon or hen and draw all moisture from it, as was said before; keep that water also.

<margin>6</margin>
The **sixth water** is this: Take all the **bones** of the capon or hen, pound them small with a wooden pestle in a stone mortar, and set it in a Mary’s bath. Afterward, however, place it in ashes and distill it as was said before. Keep that water separately as well.

The seventh water is this. Take the first, fifth, and sixth waters; **distill** them together through an **alembic** <note>original: "Alembicum"; a distillation head or cap</note>, and preserve this with great diligence. The **rectification** <note>the process of refining or purifying a substance through repeated distillation</note> through the ashes of the aforementioned distilled waters is this: Take gold prepared as was just recently described, and **coagulate** <note>to thicken or solidify</note> it until it is a naturally moist thing in all ways and color, just as if it were a yellow **orpiment** <note>original: "Auripigment"; a lemon-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment and alchemical ingredient</note>. And add a half-portion of its first water to it, and it will be immediately **dissolved** by that water and the three aforementioned waters. Therefore, take as much of it as a silver spoonful, and mix it with a good portion of the very best **wine** that you can obtain. In winter time, give it to a person who is **phlegmatic** <note>original: "Flegmaticus"; one of the four temperaments, characterized by being cold and moist</note>, for it preserves their health wonderfully. But if the person is **choleric** <note>original: "Colericus"; the temperament characterized by being hot and dry</note>, give it to them in pure water. To the **melancholic** <note>original: "Melancolico"; the temperament characterized by being cold and dry</note>, give it with a cabbage broth. But you should not give this water to the **sanguine** <note>original: "Sanguineo"; the temperament characterized by being hot and moist</note>; instead, give them the following water. Take a spoonful of the golden water, put it into wine, and give it to them; thus they shall be safe and free from all sickness.

In summer, however, one should give it to the **phlegmatic** in a chicken broth boiled with **parsley**; to the **choleric**, give it in another broth; and to the **melancholic**, you should give it as the time requires, in the great effects of heat or cold. But if you wish to give it to a sick person, give them the second part of the gold that is dissolved in the second "capon-water" <note>likely a distilled broth made from a castrated rooster</note>. If they are **phlegmatic**, give them half a spoonful with two parts water, and you should also give it in this way to a **sanguine** person. To the **choleric**, give two spoonfuls of the third water, and the same to the **melancholic**; thus they shall recover within three days from every sickness, however great or concerning it may be. And when you wish to give the medicine to someone with the aforementioned waters, you should ask the sick person what food they most desire; even if that food might normally be bad for them, you should nevertheless give it to them, along with a portion of this medicine the size of a pea. For this medicine helps against all natural or accidental, hot or cold illnesses.

And if you wish to travel across the country and want to have the medicine with you, you should **coagulate** it and put it in a glass; when you wish to use it, prepare it with moisture from the **potable gold** <note>original: "Auro potabili"</note>. Therefore, when you prepare **vegetables** <note>original: "Vegetabilia"; here referring to "vegetative" or life-giving alchemical medicines</note> or restorative medicines as said before, take a "vegetable" and put gold leaves into each glass, and seal the glass very tightly so that the scent cannot escape. Place it in the ashes with a gentle fire, and the gold will dissolve in seven days. If you then wish to make **potable gold** <note>original: "Aurum potabile"; a fabled liquid gold remedy believed to grant longevity</note>, draw all the watery substance from the gold with a gentle fire, and place it with the **cucurbit** <note>original: "Cucurbita"; the gourd-shaped lower vessel of a still</note> in a **Mary’s bath** <note>original: "Balneum Marie"; a gentle water bath or bain-marie</note>, and it will dissolve in four days. And that is the true **potable gold**. You should put that into a small **cucurbit**, and for every half-ounce of gold, add 100 **lots** <note>a historical unit of weight</note> of **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurij"; quicksilver</note> that has been well washed with vinegar and salt; thus in eight days, it all turns into true gold. And if you wish to **sublimate** or exalt this further, take from the **potable gold**...

## The Forty-Third Chapter
### *Of the final Potable Gold.* <note>original: "Auro potabili"; a legendary alchemical medicine consisting of gold dissolved in a liquid state so it could be swallowed.</note>

You should take one **lot** <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 1/32nd of a pound or about 15 grams</note> of **refined** gold, and beat it thin, as is done when one uses it for gilding, and cut it into very small pieces. Add well-purified **mercury** to it, and make an **amalgam** <note>original: "Amalgama"; a blend of mercury and another metal</note>. Rub this on a mortar stone with as much sulfur as is needed, or as much as the amalgam weighs. When it has been rubbed enough, place it in a crucible over the fire, and it will burn away quickly. Then rub it again on a marble stone and return it to the fire until it begins to turn red. Afterward, take the crucible out, and you will find your gold **calcined** <note>original: "Calciniert"; reduced to a fine powder through dry heat</note> in the very best way into a beautiful yellow powder, like saffron. Afterward, give it a flaming fire in a **reverberatory furnace** <note>original: "Referber ofen"; a furnace that reflects or "reverberates" the heat onto the substance being heated</note>, and then it is prepared. Wash it then with a **water of life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> that has been **distilled** four times, and let it dry out. From this powder, take as much as you wish and pour **rectified water of life** over it to the height of three fingers. Place it in a **circulating vessel** <note>original: "Circulatorium"; a glass vessel designed for continuous distillation where the vapor condenses and flows back into the base</note> in a **Mary's bath** <note>original: "Balneum Marie"; a water bath or double-boiler used for gentle, even heating</note> with half the portion of its stones <note>likely mineral catalysts or "boiling stones" to regulate the reaction</note>, and the gold will **dissolve** into a yellow water, such that it even stains a person's skin red. Afterward, draw out all the **elements**, as was said before, and protect each one from the air. For it has such virtue and power that it is impossible to describe.

The **oil** <note>original: "Oleum"</note>, however, is good for young men, as it maintains them in their strength and handsome appearance when they use it in their food. It does not let the blood rot, nor the **phlegm** <note>original: "Flegma"</note> dominate, nor the **choler** <note>original: "Cholera"</note> become overheated, nor the **melancholy** <note>original: "Melancoli"</note> increase or take the upper hand.
