### The Forty-Fourth Chapter / Of a compound Water of Life for people who are of a cold constitution, or who live in cold lands.

Since we have said enough and more in the previous chapters about the simple **Water of Life**, it is now time to explain something about the compound version—even if it is not strictly necessary. But because an intelligent person may wonderfully desire many different things, it did not seem wrong to us to reveal something of the **Compound Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite Composita"</note>, which we have undertaken to describe and teach how to assemble in many ways from various ingredients. Therefore, we first want to attend to those that should be given to the sick who are of a **cold constitution** <note>original: "Kalter Complexion"; in the medical theory of the time, a "cold" person was thought to have an excess of phlegm and lacked the "innate heat" necessary for health</note>, or whose sickness arises from cold causes. You may also use it in cold times and countries, and it is this: Take white **ginger**, cinnamon sticks, fresh **cubebs** <note>a spicy berry related to black pepper</note>, cleaned **cloves**, **nutmeg**, select **mace** <note>the lacy outer skin of the nutmeg seed</note>, **cardamom**, **zedoary** <note>an aromatic root similar to ginger</note>, **galangal** <note>a pungent root used to aid digestion</note>, and **long pepper**—take an equal amount of each. Crush them all coarsely and pour onto one part of these spices six parts of simple Water of Life. Put it into a long **cucurbit** <note>a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</note>, place a **blind alembic** <note>original: "Alembicum Cecum"; a still-head without a spout, used to circulate vapors back down rather than collecting them</note> upon it, and set it to **digest** <note>to steep in gentle heat</note> for eight days. Afterward, distill it in a **water bath** <note>original: "Balneo Marie"; literally "Mary's Bath," a vessel of hot water used to heat the glass gently and evenly</note> with a very gentle fire. Pour the **dregs** <note>original: "feces"; the solid remains</note> back onto the distilled matter and digest it again for another eight days.

# LIIIX On the Secrets of Nature

Yet some do not distill through the **Alembic** <note>An early alchemical still consisting of a gourd-like container and a cap with a downward tube.</note>; however, it is better if you do distill it that way. And when you distill it three times, the first water is called **Blessed Water** <note>original: "Aqua benedicta"</note>; the second is called **Compound Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vitæ composita"</note>; the third is called **Balsam Water** <note>original: "Aquam Balsami"</note>. For its composition, take sage leaves, **Rue** <note>original: "Ruta"</note>, fresh **Beaver Musk** <note>original: "Bibergeil"; castoreum, a substance from beaver glands used for its strong scent and medicinal properties.</note>, **Lemon Peel** <note>original: "Corticis citri"</note>, laurel berries, lavender flowers, and rosemary flowers—three *quintlin* <note>A unit of weight roughly equal to 4 grams.</note> of each. You shall distill these things with the previously described spices, and always pour six parts of **Simple Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vitæ simplicis"; likely a high-proof distilled alcohol.</note>—that is, the unmixed kind—over one part of the spices.

<margin>For headaches.</margin>
This **Water of Life** is good against all illnesses of the head that come from **phlegmatic moisture** <note>According to the theory of the four humors, an excess of "phlegm" was thought to be cold and wet, causing sluggishness or congestion.</note>, if one takes two *quintlin* of it in the morning with two *lot* <note>A weight of approximately 15–16 grams.</note> of the very best wine. However, some soak a slice of bread in this water. Others smear or moisten the head with one *lot* of this water; add to it two *lot* of **Betony** water. But take care that you do not use this for illnesses and pains of the head that come from **heat** <note>Such as fevers or inflammatory conditions.</note>, unless you take another "cold" medicine with it that might temper the heat of this water.

<margin>Memory.</margin>
This water also creates a good memory if one drinks it for several mornings on an empty stomach—half a *quintlin* mixed with rosemary water. And one should smear the head with it at the back of the neck and let it dry on its own.

<margin>For madness.</margin>
It is also good for those who are "mad" in the head or foolish, which is called **Mania** <note>original: "Maniam"</note>. If one mixes it with **Marjoram** and rosemary waters and wets cloths in it, then wraps them around the head against the skin, it works wonderfully.

<margin>Stroke.</margin>
This compound water is also good against **Palsy** <note>original: "Parle"; a condition involving tremors or loss of motor function.</note> or **Stroke** <note>original: "Schlag"</note> if one mixes it with sage water and washes the limbs with it often, or if one drinks it every day mixed with lavender water.

<margin>For toothache.</margin>
It is good for "lentil-marks" and signs, or spots and blemishes on the face, and for all defects or pains of the eyes. It also helps wonderfully for all pains of the teeth.

<margin>For scurf.</margin>
And if one wets a comb or brush in it and combs or brushes oneself with it, the hair will not turn gray, or only very slowly. And if the head were **scurfy** <note>original: "grindig"; affected by scabs, dandruff, or scalp sores.</note>, this drives it away and heals it quickly. It also drives away or kills earwigs; it strengthens and warms a **cold stomach** <note>A "cold" stomach was believed to cause poor digestion and gas.</note> if one drinks it with wine and smears the stomach with it.

<margin>Hearing.</margin>
It also brings back the hearing if one puts it into the ear with a piece of cotton. Wounds washed with it heal wonderfully, and it does not allow [excess] flesh to grow therein.

<margin>For poison.</margin>
It is good against all poison if one drinks it on an empty stomach. Also against cancer and all fistulas if one smears them with it; it is against dropsy and the stone in the bladder. Also, if a woman cannot **conceive** <note>original: "entpfahen"</note> because of coldness, and drinks this water on an empty stomach, she shall soon conceive. If a woman is unable to have her **regular sickness** <note>original: "zimliche Kranckheit"; a common euphemism for menstruation.</note>, one should give it to her in this water with **Galangal** <note>original: "Galgen"; a root in the ginger family.</note> and **Gentian**, or soak a piece of cotton in it and smear the **womb** <note>original: "Mutter"</note> with it; it helps. Also, if one pours this over fish or meat and other food, it [neither] rots nor...

It focuses on the "warming" properties of these distilled spirits to counteract "cold" humors and conditions like palsy, madness, and infertility.</summary>

XLIIII
Philippus Ulstadius.

It does not spoil / nor is it touched by flies. When a wine turns to vinegar / or is already sour / if one pours this water into it / the wine regains its original substance / and nature / And when one moistens soft sugar with it / it becomes hard. <margin>For jaundice.</margin> It is also good against **jaundice** <note>original: "Gelbesucht"; literally "yellow-sickness," caused by liver dysfunction.</note> / if a person rubs themselves with it / against the trembling of all limbs / and against the stench of the mouth / or nose. Also, if a person cannot digest their food / they should moisten a cloth in it / and lay it upon the stomach / it helps wonderfully. If a person has a **cramp** / moisten a cloth in it / and lay it upon the chest. <margin>For lumbago.</margin> Whoever has **lumbago** <note>original: "Lendeweh"; pain in the lower back or loins.</note> / should drink of it often. It is also good against the **falling sickness** <note>original: "fallen Siechtagen"; a historical term for epilepsy.</note> / also against the **smallpox** / or blemishes on the face / and against **warts** <note>original: "Feigwarzen"; literally "fig-warts," often referring to genital warts or similar skin growths.</note>. **Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a medieval scholar and friar whose works on natural science and alchemy were highly influential.</note> praises it most highly / because of its miraculous effects against <margin>For palsy</margin> **palsy** <note>original: "Pårlin"; likely a contraction of "Paralyis," referring to various forms of paralysis or strokes.</note>.

# The Forty-Fifth Chapter / On
## a Compound Water of Life that Emperor
## Frederick the Third made
## and used.

In 4 pounds of the most highly **rectified** <note>Distilled multiple times to reach high purity and alcohol content.</note> **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol.</note> / 4 pounds of **Malvasia** <note>original: "Maluasiers"; a sweet, strong Mediterranean wine highly prized in the Renaissance.</note> / 6 **lot** <note>A unit of weight roughly equal to 15–16 grams.</note> of cinnamon sticks / 2 *lot* of cloves / 2 and a half *lot* of white ginger / 2 *lot* of nutmeg / mace / 1 *lot* of **zedoary** <note>original: "Zitwen"; an aromatic root similar to ginger but more bitter.</note> / 2 **quintlin** <note>A smaller unit of weight, about 4 grams.</note> of **galangal** <note>original: "Galgen"; a root related to ginger used for digestion.</note> / **cubebs** <note>original: "Cubeben"; a type of "tailed pepper" used as a spice and medicine.</note> / **hyssop** / lavender flowers / white roses / sage / 1 *lot* of each / 1 *lot* of **herb bennet** <note>original: "Benedicten wurzel"; also known as wood avens, used as a tonic.</note> root / **feverfew** <note>original: "Mutterkraut"; used traditionally for headaches and fevers.</note> / **water mint** <note>original: "Bachmünz"</note> / 1 *lot* of each. Crush all these things together / and put them into a **cucurbita** <note>The gourd-shaped base of an alchemical still.</note> large enough that 15 or 16 pounds may fit inside / and add 6 *lot* of white sugar / 7 *lot* of small raisins / 7 *lot* of fat figs / and 1 *lot* of camphor / 2 pounds each of rose water / endive water / and elderflower water / put it all into a *cucurbita* / **lute** <note>To seal the joints of the vessel with a clay-like paste to prevent air from entering or vapors from escaping.</note> it as well as possible / and place it in the sun for 15 days / specifically for ten days before **St. John’s Day** <note>June 24th, the summer solstice, a significant day for harvesting medicinal herbs and alchemical operations.</note> / and ten days after. Afterward, strain the water / and distill it through an **alembic** <note>The cap and tube portion of a still that cools and collects the vapor.</note> / and keep it in a dry place / And be careful / that no woman who has her **monthly sickness** <note>original: "jhr Kranckheit"; a common Renaissance medical superstition held that the presence of a menstruating woman would spoil alchemical "spirits."</note> / touches the water or handles it. Its weight or measure is half a spoonful / taken on an empty stomach. It is exceptionally good for a **cold stomach** <note>In the humoral system, a "cold" stomach was thought to cause poor digestion and lethargy.</note> / and maintains the whole body in its health.

## Chapter Forty-Five / On a Compound Water of Life / which Emperor Frederick III made and used.

Take 4 pounds of uniquely **rectified** <term>Rectificiertes: purified and strengthened through repeated distillation</term> **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"</note> / 4 pounds of **Malmsey wine** <term>Maluasiers: a sweet, potent Greek wine highly prized in the Middle Ages</term> / 6 **lot** <note>A historical unit of weight, approximately 15-16 grams.</note> of **cinnamon sticks** <note>original: "Zimetrör"</note> / 2 **lot** of **cloves** <note>original: "Negelin"</note> / 2.5 **lot** of white **ginger** <note>original: "Ingber"</note> / 2 **lot** of **nutmeg** <note>original: "Muscatnuß"</note> / **mace** <note>original: "Muscatblüht"</note> / **zedoary** <note>original: "Zitwen"; a type of pepper.</note> / **hyssop** <note>original: "Isop"</note> / **lavender flowers** / white **roses** / **sage** / 1 **lot** of each / **herb bennet root** <note>original: "Benedicten wurtzel"; Wood Avens.</note> 2 **lot** / **motherwort** <note>original: "Mutterkraut"</note> / **water mint** <note>original: "Bachmünz"</note> / 2 **lot** of each / Crush all these things together / and put them into a **gourd** <term>Cucurbita: a gourd-shaped glass vessel used as the base of a still</term> large enough / that 15 or 16 pounds may fit inside / and add to it 6 **lot** of white sugar / small raisins / fat figs / 7 **lot** of each / and 1 **lot** of **camphor** <note>original: "Canfer"</note> / **rose water** / **endive water** / **elderflower water** / 2 pounds in total / put it all into a **gourd** / seal it well with **lute** <note>original: "verlutier"; a paste used to hermetically seal laboratory apparatus.</note> / and place it in the sun for 15 days / Namely for ten days before Saint John's Day / and ten days after <note>St. John's Day is June 24; this period around the summer solstice was considered the most potent time for herbal preparations.</note>. After that, strain the water / and distill it through an **alembic** <term>Alembicum: the cap of a still that cools vapors into liquid</term> / and keep it in a dry place / And be careful / that no woman who has her monthly illness <note>A common medieval superstition that a menstruating woman's presence or touch would spoil an alchemical or medicinal process.</note> / touches the water or handles it. Its weight or measure is half a spoonful / taken while fasting. It is exceedingly good for a **cold stomach** <note>In humoral medicine, a "cold stomach" was thought to cause poor digestion and sluggishness.</note> / and maintains the whole body in its health.

# XLVII
->On the secrets of Nature<-

Also, take 24 lots <term>Lot: a pre-metric unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</term> of **Sage** / **Nutmeg** / **Cloves** / **White ginger** / **Grains of paradise** / **Cinnamon** / 4 quintlin <term>Quintlin: also known as a dram, about 4 grams</term> of each / 2 lots of **laurel oil** / 1 quintlin of fresh **castoreum** <note>original: "Bibergeil"; a pungent secretion from beaver sacs used historically to treat nervous system disorders</note>. **Spikenard** <note>original: "Spice Indice"; an aromatic essential oil from the Himalayas</note> / **Rosemary** / half a quintlin of each. **Rosemary flowers** 1 quintlin / **Rue leaves** 2 lots / **Marjoram leaves** 1 quintlin / **Bark** <note>original: "Corticis"; Latin for bark, likely referring to cinnamon or cinchona bark</note> 2 quintlin. All these things should be fresh / but if they cannot be obtained fresh / then crush the old ones into a powder / and pour the very best **white wine** over them / that you can find / thereafter place it in a **digestion furnace** to **putrefy** <note>original: "Putrificieren"; in alchemy, this refers to a process of slow fermentation or breaking down organic matter with gentle heat</note> for one month / This digestion should happen in a **bain-marie** <note>original: "Balneo Marie"; a water bath used for gentle heating</note> with a heat of the first degree. Thereafter distill it in a bain-marie through an **alembic** <note>original: "Alembicum"; a classic distillation apparatus with a cooling "beak"</note> / And when they are distilled / pour it back onto the **dregs** <note>original: "Feces"; the solid residue left after distillation</note> / distill it again in a bain-marie / and pour it back onto the dregs. But for the third time / distill it again through the **ashes** <note>original: "durch die äschen"; a method of distillation using a sand or ash bath for higher, more even heat</note> / and keep it very well in a tightly sealed glass / for it has these following virtues / or powers. Any food sprinkled with it / retains its flavor / And it makes the wine it is put into taste good. It drives away the evil poisonous air / heals all pains / and ailments of the **eyes** / it also heals all **wounds** that are washed with it / It warms the **dropsy** <note>original: "Wassersucht"; an old term for edema or swelling caused by fluid retention</note> / it also helps against all pains of the **lungs** / of the **spleen** / the **rectum** and the **intestines** / and of the **head**. It drives away all spots of the face / and the stench of the **mouth** / and the **nose**. It also soothes the pain of the **teeth** / and makes one digest well / it dissolves and expels **clotted or stagnant blood**. It strengthens the **memory** wonderfully / It also **rejuvenates** a person / and takes away all sadness. It is also against **jaundice** / and it sometimes loosens the intestines or **loins** / if they have become constricted / it colors **copper** into a silver color. It takes away all **trembling** of the limbs / and **stammering or stuttering**. It causes **hollow teeth** to fall out on their own / and drives the **worms** out of the body. Also, if an **adder or snake** has pecked or bitten a person / and the wound has become purulent / it heals it quickly. It takes away all tightness and **congestion of the chest** / and heavy breathing / or wheezing. It is quite powerful against the **stone in the bladder** / drives away the **cough** / and makes a good **voice**. It transforms the **infertility** of women into fertility / makes the **deaf** hear. It dissolves all **abscesses** / sores / mange / and others of the same kind. It also takes away **leprosy** / if it is not too old or deep-rooted. All swelling of the **throat and neck** it heals. And it has all the virtues and powers of **natural balsam** / for it preserves and maintains a person wonderfully in their health. But those who are of a **hot nature** <note>referring to the Four Humors; people with "choleric" or "sanguine" temperaments were thought to be "hot"</note> / should not use much of it / but rather the **Phlegmatici** <note>people of a "cold and moist" phlegmatic temperament</note> / And its use is / to be taken while fasting / according to your pleasure.

## The Forty-Seventh Chapter / Another Compound Water of Life.

<page-num>XLV</page-num>
<header>Philipp Ulstad.</header>

<margin>For the plague.</margin>
Take **Cold Pearl Powder** <note>original: "Pulueris Dyamargariti frigidi"; a traditional cooling remedy made from crushed pearls</note> / **Amber Powder** / **Sweet Musk Powder** / 1 quintlin <term>Quintlin: also known as a dram, approximately 4 grams</term> of each / **Galen’s Powder of Joy** <note>original: "Pulueris leticie Galieni"; a medieval compound meant to lift the spirits and treat melancholy</note> / **The Abbot's Rose Powder** <note>original: "Dyarhodo Abbatis"</note> / **Three-Sandalwood Powder** / 2 quintlin of each / **The Liberating Powder** / **Selected Bezoar Powder** <note>original: "Pulueris electi bezoardici"; Bezoars were stony masses found in the stomachs of goats, prized as universal antidotes to poison</note> / 3 quintlin of each / **Great Theriac of Andromachus or Galen** <note>Theriac was a famous ancient poly-pharmaceutical "wonder drug" containing dozens of ingredients, including opium and viper flesh</note> 2 lot <term>Lot: a pre-metric unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</term> / **Best Mithridatum** <note>Another ancient semi-mythical antidote similar to Theriac</note> 2 lot / **Tormentil** / **Dittany** / equal parts / **Angelica-Burnet** / **Birthwort** / 3 quintlin of each. **Prepared Armenian Bole** <note>original: "Boli armeni"; a medicinal red clay from Armenia used as an astringent</note> / **Sealed Earth** <note>original: "Terre sigillate"; medicinal clay pressed into cakes and stamped with a seal to prove authenticity</note> / 2 lot of each / **Rhubarb** <note>original: "Rauedseni"; likely a variation of Rheum/Rhabarbarum</note> 2 quintlin / **Spodium** <note>original: "Spodij"; bone ash or metallic oxides</note> / **Ivory shavings** / 1 quintlin of each / **Vomiting Nut** <note>original: "Nucis vomice"; the seeds of the Strychnos nux-vomica tree, containing strychnine</note> / three in number / 6 pounds of **Rectified Water of Life** <note>distilled alcohol</note>. Set it all to **digest** <note>a slow infusion using gentle heat</note> for eight days / thereafter distill it in a **bain-marie** <note>original: "Balneo Marie"; a water bath</note> through an **alembic** / with a strong fire. But when the water is distilled / then add into it / **Fresh Alexandrian Musk** / **Good Ambergris** / one scruple <term>Scruple: a small unit of weight, about 1.3 grams</term> of each. **Oriental Saffron** / that is / saffron from the East / tied up in a little silk cloth / though it should be uncrushed / 2 quintlin / half a pound of white sugar / 8 lot of **Syrup of Stoechas** <note>original: "Syrupi sticados"; syrup made from French Lavender</note> / 4 lot of **Licorice Syrup** / 1 lot of **Fresh Cassia Fistula** <note>the pulp of the Golden Shower Tree, used as a laxative</note> recently taken from the pod / and **Unicorn** <note>original: "Einhorn"; likely ground narwhal tusk, highly valued for its supposed anti-poison properties</note> one-third of a quintlin. The **Armenian Bole** shall be prepared thus: Take it powdered / and mix it with these waters / **Sorrel** / **Roses** / an equal amount of each / two parts of **Scabious water** / **Burnet water** / **Coltsfoot water** <note>original: "aque Ungule Caballine"</note> / equal parts / Mix it together / and make a dough with the Armenian Bole / and let it dry out / Thereafter crush or rub it / and make it into a dough again with the aforementioned waters / do this three times / rub it and **impaste** it / that is / make it into a dough / then you have a prepared Armenian Bole. This water has the most wonderful effect against the plague (as has been proven by many) if it is given to someone with **Scabious water** / that is / **Aposteme-herb water** <note>original: "Apostem Krautwasser"; Scabious was used to treat apostemes, or abscesses</note> / **Sorrel water** / and **Coltsfoot water** <note>original: "Brañlattichwasser"</note>. It also has a very great effect against all pains of the head / sides or loins / and the kidneys / which arise from bad blood. It also cleanses the lungs / strengthens the heart / and drives all poison from the same / But those who are under forty years of age or even younger / should not use this water often / because of its great heat.

## The Forty-Ninth Chapter /
### Another recipe.

Take four pounds of simple **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note> / that has been distilled only once / **Cloves** / **white Ginger** / **Rosemary** / one lot <term>Lot: a pre-metric unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</term> of each / crush it all together / but only coarsely / and then put it into that same Water of Life / and let it putrefy in **horse dung** <note>Horse manure was frequently used by early chemists as a source of steady, gentle heat to encourage fermentation or "digestion"</note> for eight days in a **digestion vessel** / Afterward, distill it through an alembic in a Mary’s Bath / pour it back onto the **dregs** <note>original: "Feces"; the solid residue left after distillation</note> / and digest it for a second time / And for the third time ferment it / and distill it in the same manner / as was said above / and then it is ready. This Water is good against all the infirmities of a person / and pains that come from the cold. It is also good against all pains of the chest / belly / and the stomach. And if a stout person would like to become thin / let them use this Water every morning while fasting. But if a thin person would like to become stout / let them use it mixed with sugar / and after two months you shall see its effect.

...bread and pour three or four drops of the aforementioned water upon it / it strengthens the heart wonderfully / and also takes away quite exceedingly quickly all illnesses of the head / and of the entire body / Also all superfluities <note>Excessive bodily fluids or "humors" believed to cause disease</note> of the whole body / whether they be from cold or heat / and it preserves or maintains all limbs of the human in the very best way. Young people, however, who are still under thirty years of age / should use it seldom / unless they are sick / But those thirty years old may use it every day / and the older one is / the better it suits them / because it strengthens wonderfully all natural power / so that a person may well live to the goal / set for them by God / and reach it with good ability / provided they otherwise keep a good regimen in their life / since it has the power and virtue of all other medicines or remedies. You may use it at every meal / by putting four or six drops into another wine. This is the true and genuine **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"</note> / which has a noble smell and taste above all others. It is also quite certain from the confirmation of many highly learned physicians / that the scent alone preserves a person unharmed from poisoned air / And therefore they advise / that one should use it every day during times when a general dying or plague <note>original: "Pestilenz"</note> reigns / before one enters a house / because there is no medicine / that maintains the natural warmth more than this **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"</note>.

It truly has an affinity with nature / therefore it is converted into blood and natural warmth / And on whichever day one uses it / three or four drops while fasting / they are safe from a stroke <note>original: "Schlag oder Tropff"; literally a 'strike' or a 'drop' of paralysis</note> / from the plague / and from the pox which is currently called the French disease <note>original: "Franzosen"; a common early modern name for syphilis</note>. <margin>The Brain.</margin> It has great power to strengthen the brain. It is also good if someone has a cold head / or is otherwise scabby or impure / if they smear the same with it. It is also good against all fluxes of the brain / and fainting. <margin>Abscesses.</margin> It checks all abscesses <note>original: "Apostemen"</note> or sores / if one lays a cloth soaked in it upon them. It drives away the "falling sickness" <note>Epilepsy</note> / if one puts cotton soaked in it into the nostrils / and rubs the pulse with it. <margin>Trembling of the limbs.</margin> It also takes away the trembling of all limbs / maintains virtue / cleanses the face / drives away wrinkles / and all spots or marks on the face. <margin>The Eyes.</margin> It makes dim eyes clear / heals running eyes if one smears them with it / also if one puts it on the eyes / it heals all ailments / and when one smears it on the temples / it drives away all evil moisture / But if you put the juice of greater celandine <note>original: "Schelkraut"; traditionally used for eye complaints</note> or rue into it / then it helps against all darkness of the eyes / if you put only two or three drops of it into the eyes. <margin>Ringing of the ears.</margin> It kills earwigs / and heals the ringing in the ears / which arises from cold / drives away deafness or hard-of-hearingness / it soothes the pain of the teeth / also heals all spitting of blood / if one uses it with nightshade juice / and with the powder of the stone **Hematite** <note>original: "Emathitis"; a reddish iron ore used as a blood-stancher</note>. It also cures palsy <note>original: "Pärlin"</note> or stroke / if one smears the temples with it / and puts it on the tongue. It also takes away all stuttering / if one gives someone twice every week some of the **Fetid Pills** <note>original: "Pillulis fetidis"; a standard medicinal preparation, often containing strong-smelling resins like asafoetida, used as a purgative</note> / in German, the "stinking pills."

->Another good <term>Aqua vitæ</term> <note>Water of Life</note> Compound.<-

<header>Philippus Ulstadius. XLVIII</header>

**For the face.** Take 1 <term>lot: a unit of weight, roughly 14 to 17 grams</term> of the aforementioned water; mix it with 2 lot of bean blossom water or pimpernel <note>original: "Bibenellen"; a herb often used for skin conditions and internal healing</note> water, and wash the face with it morning and evening. Also, drink it three or four times every week with endive water; it drives away blemishes and makes the face clear.

<margin>For fistulas</margin>
The second water is yellow in color. The third water is like a black oil, which is very good for fistulas <note>chronic, abnormal pipe-like sores or passages in the body</note>—that is, for those that are weeping—and also for cancer <note>original: "Krebsß"; referring to malignant or spreading sores</note> and other wounds. It is considered as good as true balsam. And take note that this is the good water first mentioned in the 51st Chapter.

## The Fifty-Third Chapter /
->Another Water of Life for many ailments.<-

Take one pound of the very best honey;
One and a half pounds of Water of Life <note>original: "Aqua vite"</note> that has been distilled four times;
2 <term>quintlin: a small unit of weight, roughly 3.6 to 4 grams</term> of Wood of Paradise <note>likely Agarwood or Aloeswood</note>; 2 quintlin of Gum Arabic.
Nutmeg, Galangal, fresh Cubebs, choice Cinnamon, Mace, Cloves <note>original: "Gariofeliß/ Negelin"</note>, Spikenard—of each 3 quintlin;
Fresh Alexandrian Musk <note>original: "Musci Alexandrini recentis"</note>, good Ambergris—equal parts, one-third of a quintlin each. 
Grind these into a coarse powder and put it all into a glass cucurbit <note>original: "Cucurbita"; a gourd-shaped distillation vessel</note> to digest in manure <note>original: "Mist"; a common alchemical heat source providing steady, gentle warmth</note> for eight days. Afterward, distill it with a gentle fire in a water bath <note>original: "Balneo Marie"</note>. Thus you have a Compound Water of Life, excellent for many diseases; you may use it often, as has been heard before.

## The Fifty-Fourth Chapter /
->Another Water of Life.<-

Then one should take Cloves, long pepper, Grains of Paradise—of each 2 quintlin;
Nutmeg, 6 quintlin; Mace, 2 lot;
Choice Cinnamon, white Ginger, Galangal—of each 3 quintlin;
Lavender flowers, Basil—of each 2 lot; Sage, 1 lot; Rosemary flowers, Mint, Polypody <note>original: "Engelsüß"; literally 'Angel-sweet', a type of fern root</note>, Hyssop—of each 2 quintlin; Balsam herb, 2 lot;
Wood of Paradise, 3 lot; fresh Alexandrian Musk, one-third of a quintlin. These herbs should be dried; then pour 16 pounds of rectified <note>purified through repeated distillation</note> Water of Life over them and digest in horse manure for 8 days. Afterward, distill them through an alembic <note>original: "Alembicum"; the cap or apparatus used for distilling</note> in a water bath, and keep it in a well-sealed glass. It is to be used for many diseases in the manner previously mentioned in the 47th Chapter.

## The Fifty-Sixth Chapter / Another, much more precious recipe.

Several of our German physicians have a noble, excellent Water of Life <note>original: "Aquam vite"</note> / in which they have experienced many excellent effects / to strengthen the human body / and to protect it from diseases / especially for the brain / and all internal pains of the head / that come from moisture or cold / to dissolve and drive those away / and to delight the instruments or tools of the soul / and the five senses. It strengthens the four principal organs <note>traditionally the heart, brain, liver, and lungs or kidneys</note> wonderfully / along with the kidneys / and all the intestines / It also brings a very good digestion to the stomach / and gives all external limbs a wonderful strength through its scent alone / if one mixes one part of this water / and two parts of Sage / Lavender / and Rosemary water together / and wets a sponge therein / rubbing the limbs with it morning and evening / and letting it dry by itself / Or if one puts three or four drops of it into a cup full of wine / and drinks it before the midday meal / or supper.

<margin>The Head.</margin>
It is good for strengthening the head / therefore take one <term>quintlin: a small weight, about 3.6 to 4 grams</term> of this Compound Water of Life with one lot of Peony water / this should be drunk every morning and evening / or a slice of bread dipped into it / and eaten every day on an empty stomach three hours before the meal / it certainly helps.

<margin>Brain and memory.</margin>
For the brain and memory / take one quintlin of this water. Rosemary and Marjoram water / one lot of each / use it as said before / it strengthens the brain.

<margin>The Chest.</margin>
For the chest / take one quintlin of this water. 2 quintlin of Rue <note>original: "Maurrauten"</note> water. Use it as above / it makes you "wide" <note>likely meaning it opens the airways or eases breathing</note> and strengthens the chest.

<margin>The Heart.</margin>
For the heart / take one quintlin of this water / Borage / and Bugloss <note>original: "Ochsenzungen"; literally "ox-tongue," a plant used to treat heart palpitations and melancholy</note> water / 2 quintlin of each / use this as above / it strengthens and empowers the heart.

<margin>The Stomach.</margin>
For the stomach / take one quintlin of this water / Wormwood / and Mint water / 2 quintlin / use it as above / drinking it morning and evening / or taken with bread / it strengthens and preserves [the stomach].

# Of the Secrets of Nature

...has been distilled. All the following things shall be put into the **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"; a high-proof distilled spirit used as a solvent</note> to be **digested** <note>to "digest" in alchemy means to let ingredients steep in gentle heat to extract their properties</note> for four days and four nights in a **water bath** <term>Balneo Marie: a "Mary’s Bath," or double-boiler, used for gentle heating</term>:
Sage, Rosemary, Thuja, Marjoram, Lavender, Roses, Mithridate <note>a famous ancient "universal antidote" containing dozens of ingredients</note>, Theriac <note>a complex herbal jam used against poison and plague</note>, Laurel oil, Bugloss, Borage, Masterwort, Angelica, Rhapontic rhubarb, Juniper berries, Mint, Water mint, Feverfew, Vervain, Betony, Poly-germander or "Pure-flower," and Castoreum <note>original: "Bibergeil"; a scent gland secretion from beavers used in medicine</note>.

And when they are then **putrefied** <note>in this context, it means softened or broken down by the liquid</note>, then distill them in the ashes with a fire as gentle as you can, through an **alembic** <term>alembic: a hooded cap used in distillation to collect and cool vapors</term>, so that you might count one, two, three between each drop that falls. Then, put into it: Cinnamon, Cloves, a Nutmeg, Grains of Paradise, Galangal, Mace, Calamus, Long pepper, Zedoary, Cubebs, Cardamom or **Aloeswood** <note>original: "Paradiß holz"</note>, Citrus peels, Balsam fruit, Balsam wood, Spikenard, Oak mistletoe, Peony root and its seeds, prepared Coriander, Basil seed, **Devil’s-grape seed** <note>original: "semen vue Demonis"; likely stavesacre or a similar potent botanical</note>, Leopard’s bane, etc. These things should be made into a coarse powder, put into the distilled water, and **digested** together for fourteen days. Afterward, one should distill them again in a water bath through an alembic. Add to this: Camphor, Rhubarb, Saffron, Ambergris, and Musk.

If you wish to have the water even better, add to it: **Pearl powder according to Nicolaus** <note>original: "pulueris Diamargariti secundum Nicolaum"</note>, **Iris and musk powder according to Nicolaus** <note>original: "Diapliris cum musco"</note>, **The Abbot’s rose powder according to Nicolaus** <note>original: "Diarhodon abbatis"</note>, **Musk powder according to Mesue** <note>original: "Dyamusci secundum Mesue"</note>, **Ambergris powder** <note>original: "pulueris Diambre"</note>, one **quintlin** <term>quintlin: a small unit of weight, about 3.75 grams</term> of each; **Electuary with gems powder** <note>original: "pulueris Electuarij cum gemmis"</note>, **Galen’s powder of joy** <note>original: "pulueris Leticie Galieni"</note>, and half a quintlin of **Aloeswood powder** <note>original: "pulueris Dyaligni aloes"</note>. Add to this ten or twenty **ducats** <note>gold coins</note> of the very best gold, with half a pound of white sugar, and they shall stand for three days in lukewarm water in a water bath. Afterward, one should filter it through a felt.

<image-desc>A circular woodcut diagram containing two retorts (distillation vessels) arranged vertically, with the neck of the upper vessel descending towards the lower one, illustrating a specific method of filtration or tiered distillation via a felt strip as mentioned in the text.</image-desc>

Distill in "crooked glasses" that are called **retorts**, as you see illustrated here. And one should stand higher than the other. Also, one should cut small strips out of **felt** and hang them inside, so the **matter** <note>the liquid medicine</note> distills through them. However, the glasses should be **luted** <note>sealed with airtight paste or clay</note> in the best way possible, so that nothing can evaporate. And this distillation is the very best and surpasses all others. Some physicians have made a **Green Water** to strengthen the stomach, and used the same with the aforementioned Water of Life, or with the good **Claret** <note>a spiced, sweetened medicinal wine</note> that follows—as a certain **Count Palatine** <note>a high-ranking German prince</note> once did.

<margin>Making the green water.</margin>
The Green Water is made thus: Take four pounds of Water of Life that has been distilled four times through an alembic in a water bath; six **lot** <term>lot: a weight of about 15 grams</term> of dried Feverfew; four lot of Balsam-herb; pour the Water of Life over them and let everything rest thus for eight days, then use it. And note: one should not dry the herbs in the sun, but in the air, otherwise the water will become black.

<margin>Making Claret.</margin>
But the Claret is made thus: Take four pounds of the very best white wine; eight lot of white hard sugar; two lot of select Cinnamon; three quintlin of prepared Coriander; two quintlin of Cloves; half a quintlin each of Grains of Paradise and white Ginger; and one...

...a third part of a quint <term>quintlin: a small weight, roughly 4 grams</term>. **Zedoary** <note>original: "Zitwen"; an aromatic root similar to ginger</note> one-half quint. Crush it all into a fine powder. Afterward, it should be passed through or strained, just as one makes **Hypocras** <note>original: "Ipocras"; a spiced and sweetened wine filtered through a cloth "sleeve" or strainer</note>. Keep it in an inner vessel. Some use or consume this with the aforementioned **Water of Life** <note>original: "Aqua vite"; a concentrated distilled spirit used as a medicinal base</note>.

<margin>Memory.</margin>
These are the virtues or powers of this aforementioned water. It is good for the **memory** if one drinks one **lot** <term>lot: a weight roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15–16 grams</term> of it every day, mixed with one lot of **Rosemary**, **Marjoram** water, and **Melissa** or **Feverfew** water, two quints of each.

<margin>Head-frenzy.</margin>
For **head-frenzy**, called **Mania** <note>in the 16th century, mania was often attributed to an imbalance of "humors" or temperatures in the brain</note> when it arises from coldness: take one lot of this water, and two quints each of **Betony** and **Pennyroyal** water. Mix them together and soak a linen rag in it, and wrap it around the head. Or, let him drink two quints of this water every day with three quints of Betony water. He should also take equal parts of **Golden Pills** <note>original: "pillulas Aureas"; a common laxative and tonic of the time</note> and **Pills of Cochia** <note>original: "pillulas Cochie"; a standard purgative pill</note>, one-third of a quint, and make pills with Betony water, taking five of them every day when he goes to sleep. Or when he rises, **Dianthos** in tablets <note>a rosemary-flower confection used for the heart and head</note>, one quint, which settles the frenzy of the head.

Likewise, take one lot of the aforementioned water, three quints each of **Lavender** and **Sage** water, and every day take **Diapliris with Musk** <note>original: "Diapliris cum Musco"; a medicinal powder likely containing Orris root (Iris)</note> according to Nicolaus <note>referring to Nicolaus Salernitanus, a famous medieval medical authority</note>, for **dizziness**.

<margin>Dizziness.</margin>
<margin>Bald Head.</margin>
For a **bald head**: likewise, take **White Lily** water and **Aaron’s Beard** <note>original: "barba Aaron"; also known as Cuckoo-pint, which the author notes is "German Ginger"</note> water, one-half quint of each. Take one lot, or six quints, of this [aforementioned] water; add to it one quint of **Oil of Tartar** <note>original: "Weinstein öl"; a solution of potassium carbonate</note> and smear the head with it; then hair will grow there.

<margin>Face / to make clear.</margin>
Likewise, take one part **Bean-flower** water and two parts of the aforementioned water; mix them together and wash the face with it once every day. You may also drink one part of this aforementioned water and two parts of the very best wine every day on an empty stomach; this is also good for the same purpose. Likewise, put this water every morning and evening into the **ears**; then lie down on that ear for a quarter of an hour so that it flows out again. Or, take equal parts of this water and the very best wine; drink that every day on an empty stomach; it drives away **deafness** or ailments of the head.

<margin>Good Hearing.</margin>
Likewise, take one lot of the mentioned water, two quints each of **Rue** juice and **Peach-leaf** juice. If you cannot obtain the juice, then take the distilled water thereof, and add a little **Aloe-patico** <note>a type of medicinal aloe</note> rubbed into a fine powder. Drop that every morning and evening into the ear and lie on the other side.

<margin>Earworms.</margin>
But after a little while, lie on the ear where the **worms** are <note>it was a common historical belief that physical worms caused earaches or tinnitus</note>; they will crawl out immediately and die. Likewise, take one lot of the mentioned water, and one quint each of **Fennel** water, **Pennyroyal**, and **Valerian** water; put them into the **eyes**; it helps.

<margin>For blood in the eyes.</margin>
Likewise, take eighteen lots of **white wine** and two lots of **Pellitory** <note>original: "Bertram"; Pellitory-of-Spain, used to stimulate saliva</note>; boil them together, and take one part of this and one part of the aforementioned water. Hold this mixture in the mouth for a while, and afterward take a fresh mouthful.

<margin>For swollen gums.</margin>
Do this often; it will benefit the **gums**.

<margin>For a stinking mouth.</margin>
For the **stench of the mouth** or nostrils: give him one lot of the aforementioned water every day with white wine in which two lots of **Woundwort** and **Red Roses** have been boiled, and add a little **Musk** <note>original: "Bisem"</note>. But some take four lots of the aforementioned water, **Rosemary**...

# Of the Secrets of Nature

...red rose water, one **lot** <term>lot: a historical weight, roughly half an ounce or 15–16 grams</term> each. These should be mixed together, and within them, you should hang **musk** <note>original: "Bisem"</note> in a small silk cloth, along with fine **ambergris** <note>original: "Ambra bona"; a rare substance used in perfumes and medicines</note>, six **grains** <term>gran: a tiny unit of weight, about 0.06 grams</term> of each. With this, you should wash your mouth three or four times every day at the very least. You may also drink this aforementioned water every morning; it makes your breath good.

For the **falling sickness** <note>original: "fallenden Siechtagen"; a historical term for epilepsy</note>, one should drink one lot of it every day with two lots of **peony** water. However, some do it this way: they take 
<margin>For the Stroke.</margin>
peony root and seeds, and **oak mistletoe**, equal parts of each. They boil them in very good wine and give it to the sick person along with the previously mentioned water. For the **palsy** <note>original: "Pärlin"; refers to paralysis or tremors</note> or **stroke**, one should frequently rub the limbs with the aforementioned water and drink two lots of this water every morning. But in the evening, take **diacastorium** <note>a compound medicine based on castoreum (beaver musk)</note> with white wine in which **lavender**, **sage**, and **juniper berries** (a handful of each) have been boiled, and then pour the very best wine over it; it helps well.

<margin>For speech.</margin>
Also, take one lot of the long-mentioned water with lavender, peony, and sage water (two **quints** <term>quintlin: approximately 4 grams</term> of each) and drink it. And take one quint of **Mithridatum** <note>original: "Mithridati"; a famous ancient "universal" antidote and cure-all</note> with wine in which **castoreum** <note>original: "Bibergeil"; a scent secretion from beavers used to treat nervous system disorders</note> has been boiled; it brings back speech.

<margin>For Melancholy.</margin>
Also, take one lot of the mentioned water with two lots of **bugloss** <note>original: "Ochsenzungen"; literally "ox-tongue," used to treat "black bile" or depression</note> water, **basil** water, and **melissa** or **feverfew** water (two quints of each), and **hart's-tongue** <note>a type of fern</note> water and **borage** water (one quint of each). Mix it together and drink it three hours before eating. Also take a tablet of the **Confection of Joy by Galen** <note>original: "Confection Leticie Galieni"; a medicine intended to cheer the spirit</note> or the **Gladdener of Almansor** <note>original: "Letificantis Almansoris"; another historical recipe for treating depression</note>; it makes you of good cheer.

<margin>Liver-sick.</margin>
Also, take one lot of this aforementioned water with **liverwort** water and **polypody** <note>original: "Engelsüß"; literally "angel-sweet," the common polypody fern</note> water (two lots of each). Drink two or three lots every evening, and in the morning three hours before the meal, take **Diathodon abbatis** <note>a digestive powder or "electuary" named for an "abbot"</note> according to the recipe of **Nicolaus** <note>likely Nicolaus Praepositus, a famous medieval medical author</note>; the liver will become fresh.

<margin>Dropsy.</margin>
Also, take one part of the first aforementioned water, two parts **elderflower** water, and three parts **fennel** water. Mix them together and drink one lot every time you wish to go to sleep in the evening. Take the **Great Theriac of Galen** <note>original: "Tyriace magni Galieni"; a complex polypharmaceutical used as a panacea</note> with wine in which **parsley**, fennel, **smallage** <note>original: "Eppich"; wild celery</note>, and **wall-rue** (one part of each) have been boiled. Use four parts of wine and let it boil down to one third. This should be given to the person with **dropsy** <note>original: "Wassersüchtigen"; edema or swelling caused by fluid retention</note> every three days, two or three hours before eating; give one quint of **Dialecce** according to **Mesue** <note>a digestive medicine attributed to the Persian physician Masawaih</note>. The sick person should guard himself against excessive drinking; for with this sickness, the more one drinks, the less he urinates—and vice-versa.

<margin>For the Stone.</margin>
Also, take one part of the aforementioned water. Take **saxifrage** <note>original: "Steinbrech"; literally "stone-breaker"</note> water, **winter cherry** <note>original: "Boberellen"</note> water, **gromwell** <note>original: "Milium solis"</note> water, and **radish** water (two parts of each). And one hour after he has drunk this, give him two quints of **Lithontriptic** <note>original: "Litontripon"; a medicine designed to dissolve stones in the bladder or kidneys</note>; he will recover from the stone.

# Regime of Life by
## Marsilio Ficino <note>original: "Marsilij Vicini." Marsilio Ficino (1433–1499) was an influential Italian scholar and physician. Although this text claims he lived to 116, historical records indicate he died at age 65; attributing extreme longevity to authors was a common way to validate their medical advice in the Renaissance.</note>, by which he grew old,
### reaching one hundred and sixteen years;
### essential to know.

<image-desc>A large, elaborate ornamental woodcut initial 'E' in a calligraphic Fraktur style, decorated with complex interlaced scrollwork and leaf-like flourishes.</image-desc>

Pietro d’Abano <note>original: "Petrus Apponensis" (c. 1250–1316), a famous Italian physician and astrologer.</note> says, and proves it with Aristotle, Galen, and Haly <note>original: "Hale"; likely referring to Haly Abbas, a 10th-century Persian physician.</note>, as well as with the confirmation of the astrologers, that natural life is not fixed to a specific point, but instead may be protected and sustained with medicine. Therefore, it is well to believe that the aforementioned Marsilio maintained himself in the long preservation of his life through such a regime and medicine.

<margin>Enemies of the high-minded</margin>
Likewise, he says it should be known that the high-minded, the highly learned, and those who frequently use wisdom or the arts have five types of enemies against which they should be warned. These are Phlegm, Melancholy, unchastity, staying awake late into the night, and the heavy use of strong wine; they should guard themselves against these.

<margin>High dwelling</margin>
Likewise, one should seek and arrange a high, spacious dwelling that is turned toward the sunrise, not near stagnant waters that cannot flow away. It is also harmful to dwell where there is much manure. If we must live in rustic places, however, we should make much use of sweet and warm scents.
<margin>Air.</margin>
We should also flee from air that is too cold, foggy, or that is too warm.

<margin>Rising in the morning.</margin>
Likewise, when we rise in the morning, we should gently scratch our bodies a little, comb the head with an ivory comb from the forehead backward, and then rub the neck with a clean, coarse linen cloth. And do not immediately set yourself busily to work or business for about a quarter of an hour; after that, turn yourself toward the contemplation of your work or your business.

One should also rise with the sunrise in the summertime. But in the winter, rising two or three hours before the sunrise is good for your body and your health.

<margin>Old people.</margin>
Likewise, the old and elderly people should always seek warmth in the winter, just as sheep seek the shade in the summer. They should also often seek...

<summary>This page introduces a health regime attributed to Marsilio Ficino, arguing that medicine and lifestyle can extend life. It specifically warns intellectuals against the "five enemies" of health—including melancholy and late nights—and offers advice on housing and morning routines.</summary>
<keywords>Marsilio Ficino, Pietro d’Abano, longevity, humoral theory, phlegm, melancholy, hygiene, architecture, morning routine, ivory comb</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues Marsilio Ficino’s regimen for a long and healthy life. Following the previous page's advice on choosing a high-altitude dwelling, Ficino now describes the ideal surroundings and transitions into specific dietary laws (dietetics) common in Renaissance preventative medicine.</meta>

...seek green meadows, gardens, and flowing waters, brooks, and places where little bees like to dwell, and also where one may enjoy the company of friendly, cheerful, and pious people.

<margin>Feet and excursions.</margin>
Likewise, protect the head from cold, especially the neck and also the feet <note>original: "Haupt-Füß." This likely emphasizes the "main" or "extremity" of the feet, or suggests keeping both ends of the body warm.</note>. One should also guard against sadness, against anger and fear, against isolation <note>original: "einigkeit," used here in the sense of brooding alone or lacking social stimulation.</note>, and against idleness. Also avoid everything that hinders the sight and the good sense of smell, and above all, avoid the darkness.

## Concerning Nutrition.

Likewise, above all things, guard yourself against overindulgence with excessive food. Live moderately; do not eat too early, nor delay the meal too long. Do not allow for many sudden changes in your habits.

<margin>Fresh and white bread.</margin>
Likewise, your bread should be fresh, light, choice, and white. There should always be twice as much bread as other food: when eating meat, bread should make up a third part; for fish and for moist vegetables, bread should be a fourth part. In the evening, less food should be taken than at the morning meal.

<margin>Meat of young pigs.</margin>
Likewise, the meat of young pigs and pig’s blood restores blood and flesh in a healthy person, when prepared with cloves and coriander.

<margin>Geese.</margin>
Likewise, geese raised on spelt or wheat are not harmful but healthy to eat; however, after slaughtering, the meat should be rubbed with salt and coriander prepared with vinegar, and allowed to sit for two or three days before cooking.

<margin>Venison.</margin>
Likewise, venison prepared and eaten in the same way is good; it wards off fever.

<margin>Dry foods.</margin>
Likewise, in all food intake, one should always consume more dry foods than moist ones, such as pepper, galantine <note>original: "Galrey," a savory spiced jelly or sauce used to preserve meat.</note>, and the like.

Likewise, vervain, bugloss <note>original: "Ochsenzung," literally "ox-tongue."</note>, borage, "heart-herbs," chicory water, lettuce, sow thistle, sea-grape <note>original: "Meertreubel."</note>, and almond milk should be common at your table.

One should also avoid foods that are too heating or too cooling, and those that cause belching <note>original: "grüntzen."</note> or smell strongly for a long time after eating.

<margin>Soft meat and soft fish are unhealthy.</margin>
Do not use many moist herbs or moist fruits, such as peaches, overripe fruit <note>original: "teyg Obs," referring to fruit that has become soft and doughy.</note>, and the like; even less should you use raw milk. Guard against soft meat and soft fish; but if you cannot avoid them, use strong spices upon them, such as ginger, cloves, and peppercorns.

## Common Medicines for the Human Body.

<margin>A freshly roasted egg.</margin>
Likewise, a freshly roasted egg, sipped <note>This refers to a soft-boiled or lightly roasted egg that remains liquid enough to drink.</note> with a little salt, sugar, and saffron, sopped with a little fresh bread, followed by a drink of clear wine—doing this often helps and strengthens the entire body wonderfully.

<meta>This page continues Marsilio Ficino’s *Regimen of Life*, shifting from general lifestyle advice to specific dietary recommendations. It details the medicinal properties of honey, spices, and specific meats, while providing a recipe for a restorative tonic akin to "potable gold."</meta>

# Regimen of Life

<margin>Honey.</margin>
In winter, it is very healthy to have honey brought to the table often, to be tasted with bread both before and after the meal.

<margin>Capers.</margin>
Likewise, dates, figs, raisins <note>original: "Mörrtreubel," likely referring to dried grapes or currants.</note>, and capers should be common features at your table.

<margin>Honey.</margin>
Freshly baked bread dipped in honey that has been mixed with a little wine sustains and strengthens the vital spirit of the body <note>The "vital spirit" was believed to be a subtle substance that carried life-force through the arteries.</note>.

Likewise, to make a powder: Take from the apothecary half a *lot* <note>A "lot" was a historical unit of weight, roughly 15-17 grams.</note> of Emblic Myrobalans <note>original: "Mirabolanum emblicarum," a fruit used in Ayurvedic and Renaissance medicine for digestion.</note>, one *lot* of sandalwood <note>original: "Scandalarum," likely *Santalum*.</note>, and two *lots* of saffron; mix these together. When often sprinkled upon your food, it strengthens your powers and maintains your entire body in good health.

<margin>Coriander.</margin>
<margin>Quinces.</margin>
It is always good to use coriander after a meal; one may have it coated in sugar <note>original: "mit Zuccar vberziehen." Sugar-coated seeds, or "comfits," were a popular digestive aid.</note> and keep it in use at the table. Likewise, quinces preserved with sugar are very beneficial; they digest all food, cheer the orifice of the stomach, and benefit the whole body.

<margin>Distilled wine.</margin>
Distilled wine <note>original: "Gebrannter Wein," referring to brandy or aqua vitae.</note> taken twice a week with rose julep <note>original: "Juleb rosarum," a medicinal rose syrup.</note> is not harmful; one may also rub it upon the body.

<margin>Liquorice.</margin>
It is also useful in the morning to chew or swallow liquorice, or sweet pomegranates for those who can obtain them.

<margin>Fresh ginger.</margin>
Fresh ginger eaten on an empty stomach strengthens the stomach and is very good for the intellect and the memory.

<margin>Roasted meat, such as beef, is not healthy.</margin>
Likewise, for cold foods <note>In humoral medicine, foods were classified as "hot," "cold," "moist," or "dry."</note>, you should use spices such as nutmeg, cinnamon, and saffron. And guard yourself against dry, hard food, such as roasted meats, or old beef and ox-flesh; avoid all salty things, bean-dishes—especially those that are poorly cooked—radishes, garlic, onions, shallots, and whatever is excessively heating, cooling, or drying, for that is harmful to the body.

## Concerning Meat.

<margin>New cheese.</margin>
Likewise, young chickens, capons, birds, young kids <note>original: "Rizzlin," referring to young goats.</note>, veal, and the meat of young four-footed animals, as well as fresh new cheese, are all not harmful; rather, one may well eat of them.

## Concerning Drink.

<margin>Which wine is best.</margin>
Likewise, guard yourself against cloudy wine, especially that which is thick and dark; but sweet Cyprus wine <note>original: "Zitprecht," likely referring to wine from Cyprus, highly valued for its medicinal qualities.</note>, which is clear and not too strong, yet fragrant, serves to hold the vital spirit together and is good for the internal organs, provided it is drunk in moderation, as Plato and Aristotle say <note>original: "Plato / vnd Aristoteles sagen."</note>.

The wine should also not be too light, but of such quality that it can withstand water; and should you wish to water it, this should be done a quarter of an hour before drinking. One should never begin to drink before eating. And for elderly people, the wine should not be mixed with water at all.

<margin>Potable gold.</margin>
Likewise, the following mixture is very nearly a "Potable Gold" <note>original: "Aurum potabile," a legendary alchemical medicine believed to grant longevity.</note>: Take borage flowers, bugloss <note>original: "Ochsenzung," literally "ox-tongue."</note>, and motherwort when the moon is in a good sign; then cook these things with white sugar that has been consecrated with rosewater, and for every two *lots* of this, add three gold leaves; thus it is prepared. Whoever uses this from time to time...

Philippus Ulstadius., LIII

used; this helps if one were very weak and powerless. Above all, it maintains healthy people in the well-being of their health and helps the weak recover wonderfully. Taken with gold-colored wine on an empty stomach, it strengthens the physical Spirit <note>The "physical" or "vital spirit" was a refined substance thought to circulate through the arteries, mediating between the soul and the body.</note>.

You should also make use of Zedoary <note>original: "Zitwan," a root similar to ginger used for digestion.</note>, Theriac <note>original: "Tryackers," a famous ancient medicinal paste used as a universal antidote and tonic.</note>, ambergris, and fragrant spices, which are good to have; such as saffron, cinnamon, and whatever is meant to be eaten should be used for food. And what is meant to be smelled, that you should smell or taste; it is a delight and a strengthening for the Heart.

<margin>A drink for the stomach.</margin>
A drink that strengthens the stomach and the entire body is good to make as follows: Take gold or gold coins and heat them until they glow red; thereafter, quench them in good aged wine once or three times, and then drink it. Or take fine gold leaf and place it in good wine for four or five hours, then drink it on an empty stomach, or sip it in chicken broth; used this way, it is very beneficial to the Heart, as well as the Head and the entire body.

<margin>Reason.</margin>
Likewise, take half a *quintlin* <note>A *quintlin* (or quentchen) was a small unit of weight, roughly 4 grams.</note> of Theriac twice a week; in winter with a little wine, but in summer with a little rosewater. Taken on an empty stomach, it preserves and rectifies the reason <note>original: "vernunfft," referring to mental clarity and cognitive faculties.</note>.

<margin>Gold.</margin>
Likewise, take pure spring water, in which you should quench glowing gold or gold coins two or three times; place several gold leaves in the water and keep a good portion of what is made clean. Also, mix your wine with it at times. When using this drink, it is best to eat good fresh eggs, especially the yolks; this keeps the entire Spirit of your body in good strength and protects you from weakness.

<margin>Cakes made from chickens and capons.</margin>
Likewise, take a capon <note>A castrated rooster, prized for its tender meat.</note> and a good hen; take from other chickens—from one or two—their hearts, livers, stomachs, and brains. Boil these very well with water and a little salt. Thereafter, crush them all together; then add sugar and egg yolks, and make little cakes <note>original: "Küchlin," meaning small cakes or pastilles.</note> from them. Prepare them with saffron and cinnamon, and you may even gild them with fine gold leaf. Then eat of them when you are hungry on an empty stomach, before every fourth or fifth day, and drink good clear wine upon them; this keeps you from the wasting of your body and strengthens your life.

### A refreshment of Life.

<margin>Stomach.</margin>
Likewise, take freshly baked warm bread soaked in Malmsey <note>original: "Maluasier," a sweet, potent Greek wine highly valued in the Renaissance.</note> and add powder of curled mint <note>original: "Krußbula kraut," likely *Mentha crispa*.</note> to it. Place this warm in a thin cloth high up on the stomach, mostly on the left side, and also smell it at the nose; this refreshes the physical Spirit. Democritus <note>The ancient Greek "laughing philosopher," often cited in medical texts for his longevity.</note> sustained himself for a long time with this.

Likewise, eating the bread with Malmsey is good to use in winter and is healthy; but in summer, it should be mixed with rosewater, otherwise it would be too heating.

Likewise, if your stomach rises with bitterness and a foul taste in the mouth, so that all things seem too salty to you, and for him...

Taken with gold-colored wine on an empty stomach, it strengthens the physical spirit <note>The "physical spirit" or vital spirit was thought to be a subtle substance that mediated between the soul and the body.</note>.

You should also make use of zedoary <note>original: "Zitwan," an aromatic root similar to ginger used for digestion.</note>, theriac <note>original: "Tryackers," a famous ancient polypharmaceutical electuary used as an antidote and general tonic.</note>, ambergris <note>original: "Ambra," a rare, fragrant substance from the sea used to strengthen the heart.</note>, and fragrant spices; these are good to have, such as saffron, cinnamon; and what is edible should be used with food, and what is fragrant should be smelled or tasted; it is a joy and a strengthening of the heart.

# Rule of Life, LIII

If neither eating nor drinking tastes pleasant to you, then use a rose-scented spice blend <note>original: "Aromaticum rosarum," a medicinal powder made of roses and spices.</note> mixed with rose honey, cinnamon, and green ginger; above all, use theriac <note>A famous ancient "cure-all" electuary containing dozens of ingredients.</note>, for all of these are good and defend against stomach pain. Also, quince electuary <note>original: "Latwerg," a thick medicinal paste made of fruit and honey.</note> should always be taken to remove the distaste of the stomach.

Likewise, Peter of Abano <note>original: "Petrus Apponensis" (1250–1316), a famous Italian physician and astrologer.</note> has tested this mixture as it is here composed: Take saffron, mace, and castoreum <note>original: "Castores," a yellowish secretion from the scent glands of a beaver, used to treat hysteria and nervous conditions.</note> in equal parts, crushed together and mixed with wine. If taken every time, he says that even for people whose life is slipping away, their life may be saved by it; therefore, use it from time to time, as it is beneficial.

Likewise, take four *lot* <note>A German unit of weight, roughly 15-18 grams.</note> of frankincense, two *lot* of myrrh, and half a *quintlin* <note>Approximately 3.6 grams.</note> of gold leaf. Grind these three pieces together cleanly and moisten them with good, aged wine. Form them into pills; do this when the moon is in a favorable zodiac sign. Then take a little from this treasure of pills every time on an empty stomach, followed by a small drink of wine. If it is summer time, however, take a little rose water instead of the wine.

Likewise, take eight *lot* of Chebulic myrobalans <note>original: "Mirabolanum Cheubularum," a dried fruit from India used as a digestive tonic.</note> and six *lot* of sugar; but in winter, add two *lot* of green ginger, and in summer one *lot*. Cook this gently, mixed with Emblic honey <note>original: "Honig Emblicarum," honey infused with the Emblic myrobalan or Indian gooseberry.</note>, adding six or seven gold leaves to the mixture. You should keep this and take a bite of it three hours before a meal. Used in this way, it rejuvenates life and keeps you in good form and health.

Likewise, if you eat soft, freshly baked warm bread, or toasted slices with cinnamon and plenty of sugar on top, soaked in good wine (or in a little rose water during summer), it is very beneficial. One might also pour almond milk over them. Eating this frequently creates a joyful nature and a cheerful mood; it also removes "heavy blood" <note>Refers to melancholy or a sluggish disposition.</note>.

Likewise, take eight sweet peeled almonds, eight *lot* of pine nuts <note>original: "Dannenkörner."</note>, two *lot* of cucumber seeds—crush these together—and four *lot* of pistachios and two *lot* of hazelnut kernels. Cook this with white sugar. Add one *quintlin* of green ginger, half a *quintlin* of saffron, one *scruple* <note>A small unit of weight, about 1.3 grams.</note> of musk <note>original: "Bisem."</note>, and just as much ambergris. Dissolve the sugar with motherwort and rose water; you may also mix gold leaf into it. When this electuary is used, it maintains life in complete vigor against illnesses.

Likewise, licorice juice, almond milk, starch porridge <note>original: "Amalei muß," a thick pap made from wheat starch.</note>, sugar, sea-grapes <note>original: "Meertreubel," likely referring to the plant *Ephedra*.</note>, and fennel should be common at your table. Also, using sage often warms the nature and defends against paralysis <note>original: "Paralisi."</note>.

<margin>Memory.</margin>
Likewise, chewing frankincense or eating it occasionally, as well as theriac and nutmeg—held long in the mouth—helps the memory very much and strengthens it.

Likewise, candied Chebulic myrobalan—that is, sugar and fennel crushed together, and a toasted slice of bread dipped in wine, sprinkled with the powder and eaten—strengthens and sustains life.

Likewise, sweet smells and tastes refresh the spirit; in summertime, use roses, violets, rose water, myrrh, and camphor <note>original: "Ganfer," modern German: Kampfer.</note>. However, in wintertime, use cloves <note>original: "Negelin."</note>, bitter oranges <note>original: "pomerangen."</note>, curled balsam, saffron, musk <note>original: "Bisem."</note>, ambergris, fragrant apples, and the scent of good wine. It is also good to hold a hyacinth stone <note>original: "Jacinctus." The zircon or hyacinth gemstone was believed to have cooling and protective properties when held in the mouth.</note> in the mouth; by all these things, the heart of one who is very sad is cheered. Also, the scent of the fir tree is very useful; one should also rub the body with the resin of the fir tree, mixed with olive oil <note>original: "Baumöl," literally "tree oil."</note> or wine. For it is well to be believed—since the fir tree reaches a great age and remains green even in winter—that it is also useful for lengthening the life of man.

### A purgative created to keep one young for a long time.

<margin>To remain youthful.</margin>
Take 2 *lot* <note>A German unit of weight, roughly 15-18 grams.</note> of washed aloe, 2 *quintlin* <note>Approximately 3.6 grams.</note> of emblic myrobalans <note>original: "Mirabolanum emblicarum," a species of medicinal fruit used in ancient pharmacology.</note>, and the same amount of chebulic myrobalans. Likewise, take the same amount of red roses and mastic, and soak this with Malmsey wine <note>original: "Malvasier," a sweet, potent wine originally from Greece.</note>. Then form pills from this when the moon is in a favorable position; take these in the morning as you please.

Likewise, a sad disposition is restored with song and the playing of strings. For David, with song and the sound of the harp, relieved Saul. And I, unworthy Marsilio, have experienced and proven the subtlety of song, the lute, and the clavichord, and what they are capable of, as stated above. Travel is also to be praised, whether by ship or by wagon, provided it is not harsh or unusual. It is also useful to look upon bubbling springs and clear, flowing water.

Likewise, to make a precious electuary <note>original: "Latwerg," a thick medicinal paste made of honey or sugar.</note>, take 7 *lot* of sweet almonds, pine nuts <note>original: "Dannzapffen körner."</note> washed for a day, and 4 *lot* of peanuts <note>original: "Erdnuss," likely referring to earth-nuts or tubers rather than the modern American peanut.</note> each. Take 7 *lot* of sugar candy and 1 pound of other sugar; put all this together in rose water and citron juice, in which glowing gold has previously been extinguished. Then cook it gently together and add cinnamon, red stone <note>original: "beine Rubei," possibly referring to burnt red bone or a red mineral powder like Armenian bole.</note>, red sandalwood, and red coral, 1 *quintlin* of each. Also pearls, saffron, raw silk, and finely cut *zendel* silk <note>Thin, light silk fabrics were sometimes included in "cordial" recipes as they were thought to contain refined spirits.</note>, half a *quintlin* of each; and 7 gold leaves, the weight of a barleycorn. Use this electuary from time to time, and see that for two hours before and after, no food is taken; from this, your whole body will receive great strength.

Likewise, take sugar candy with rose water and a little vinegar; one may also add a light wine. You should often wash your mouth with this water, and rub your hands and face with it; it is wonderfully good and makes you cheerful <note>original: "lüstig."</note>, however small and slight a thing it may seem.

Likewise, guard yourself against a strong purgative, against a long-lasting bout of diarrhea, against heavy sweating, against much unchastity <note>Excessive sexual activity was historically believed to deplete the "vital spirits."</note>, and against great thirst and great hunger.

Whatever work is written in the manner of an apothecary, mark the sign and order it from the pharmacy. You may also make half as much if the cost concerns you. Take one *quintlin* <note>Approximately 3.6 grams.</note> of Cheering Spices of Almansor <note>original: "Species letificans Almansoris," a medieval recipe intended to gladden the heart and combat melancholy.</note>, Gem Electuary <note>original: "Electuarium de gemmis," a medicinal paste containing powdered precious stones.</note>, and musk <note>original: "Bisem."</note> at a third of a *quintlin*. Take the same amount of Diacameron <note>original: "Dyacameron," a complex restorative medicine.</note>, 9 *lot* of fine sugar; let it all be crushed and made into a powder, like a stomach spice. This should be used twice a week, in the morning with good wine, one *quintlin* each time, for bad humors and vertigo. <margin>Vertigo of the head.</margin> Take celandine <note>original: "Schellkraut."</note>, agrimony, hops, the roots of each, and crush them into a clean powder; if you wish, you may crush them while green, and mix in eight egg whites, 22 *lot* of distilled honey, and then take scorched rye, eggshells, Roman caraway, mastic, one *lot* of each, one *quintlin* of snake grease, and crush the aforementioned pieces very cleanly. Then take as much young pig's blood as is enough to make it into an ointment, and smear yourself where you are weak. The celandine root, the agrimony, the honey water, and egg whites should be distilled separately one more time, then kept aside, and the other pieces mixed in, such as the caraway and the mastic. Take one *lot* of coriander, mace, nutmeg, cloves <note>original: "Negelin."</note>, red sandalwood, ivory shavings, grains of paradise, licorice juice, one *quintlin* of each; ginger, cubebs, galangal, long pepper, columbine seed <note>original: "Agley sat."</note>, oak mistletoe, blessed thistle seeds <note>original: "Benedicten körner."</note>, and peony roots <note>original: "Benedicten Rosenkörner wurglen."</note>, red coral, clary sage <note>original: "Scharlachkraut."</note>, betony, Roman caraway, rosemary flowers, orange peel, cinnamon bark, half a *lot* of each; one and a half *quintlin* of Dyanthos <note>A confection made of rosemary flowers.</note>, one *quintlin* of Dyambra <note>A restorative confection containing ambergris.</note>. Take the weight of 15 barleycorns each of aloeswood <note>original: "Lignum aloes."</note> and balsam wood; let everything be crushed cleanly, and mix in 7 *lot* of sugar; let it be made into a dragée <note>original: "Traget," a sugar-coated confection or lozenge.</note>.

### A good water to sharpen the sight.

<margin>For the sight.</margin>
Take agrimony, vervain, fennel, rue, and red roses, equal parts of each, and crush or bruise them in a mortar, or chop them and sprinkle them with good, sharp wine. Let it stand and putrefy in a *Balneo Marie* <note>original: "Balneo Marie," a water bath used for gentle heating in alchemy and chemistry.</note> for three days and nights. Afterward, let it be distilled in a *cucurbit* <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation.</note>. It is useful for the following: it is good for weeping eyes and takes away all pains that come from the cold. It dries and clears the tears of the eyes and drives away spots. But if you wish for it to drive away spots more effectively, then add horehound and pimpernel <note>original: "Hůnerdarm / das die roten blůmlin hat," literally "chickweed that has the red flowers."</note>, which is called in Latin *Flammula* <note>Likely referring to the Scarlet Pimpernel, *Anagallis arvensis*.</note>, and spearwort <note>original: "Brennkraut."</note>; half as much of each of these as the others. Distill it with the previous ingredients, and when it has been distilled, take myrrh, camphor, and aloe, one *quintlin* of each; bind them in a small cloth and hang it in the water. Then it is prepared correctly.

### A good compound Water of Life, which is useful for all illnesses that come from the cold, and it is made thus.

Take the roots of fennel, parsley, asparagus or yarrow, wild thistle, endive, clary sage, and wall rue; 6 *lot* of each. Mix them with enough good white wine and distill the mixture through an alembic <note>A distilling apparatus consisting of a gourd and a cap.</note>.

### To make the first powder, take Roman cumin and cinnamon bark, six *lot* of each; three *lot* of cloves; one *quintlin* of agarwood <note>original: "Paradißholz," literally "paradise wood."</note>. Ginger and pepper, four *lot* of each; galangal, cubebs, catnip, and lovage seed, two *lot* of each; one *lot* of spikenard. Let all of this be crushed into a powder and kept.

### The second powder: Take cinnamon bark, cloves, spikenard, licorice, long pepper, mastic, and yellow, white, and red sandalwood; one *lot* of each. These things should also all be crushed into a fine powder.

To the first part, take 2 parts of the first water distilled from the roots, and 2 parts of the two powders. Distill it again through a cloth bag, and add half a *maß* <note>A unit of volume, roughly 1 to 1.5 liters depending on the region.</note> of red wine. When it has been distilled through the bag, then distill it through an alembic. When it has been distilled thus, take ambergris and musk, 8 barleycorns weight of each, and hang them in the Water of Life <note>original: "Aquam vite."</note>. Cover it well and place it where there is much heat, so that it may be well tempered and blended together; then it is prepared correctly.
